QUESTION_ID: JT-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: What training tracks are available in NxtWave Intensive?
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Which job tracks do you offer?
* What courses are available?
* MERN and Java tracks matrame unnaya?
* Job track options enti?
* Which track should I choose?
* Java Full Stack vs MERN Full Stack, which is available?
* Are there any other tracks besides MERN and Java?
* What specializations are offered?
ANSWER: You can choose one of the following full-stack development tracks: Online Program: ● MERN Full Stack ● Java Full Stack Offline Training Center(in-classroom) Program: ● Python Full Stack with Gen AI ● Java Full Stack with Gen AI Across both programs, you will build projects such as: ● A portfolio website ● A food ordering application ● A social feed platform These projects help you practice real development workflows and build a portfolio you can present to recruiters.
TAGS: Job tracks, MERN Full Stack, Java Full Stack, DA track, QA track, Data Analytics, Quality Assurance, available courses, track options, track selection

QUESTION_ID: JT-002
CANONICAL_QUESTION: Are Data Analytics or Testing tracks available?
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* DA/QA tracks enduku levu?
* DA track undi aa?
* QA track provide chestunnara?
* Data Analytics track available aa?
* Quality Assurance track undi aa?
* Naku QA track kavali, possible aa?
* What is the Curriculum of the job tracks DA and QA?
ANSWER: Currently, we offer two job tracks: ● MERN Full Stack ● Java Full Stack At present, DA (Data Analytics) and QA (Quality Assurance) tracks are not available due to fewer placement opportunities in those domains. These tracks have not been offered since June 2024. If you are unsure which track to choose between MERN and Java, you don't need to decide immediately — at the end of Growth Cycle 1, you will take the Fundamentals Exam. Based on your performance, the system will suggest a track for you. You can accept that suggestion or choose your preferred track at that point.
TAGS: DA track, QA track, Data Analytics, Quality Assurance

QUESTION_ID: JT-003
CANONICAL_QUESTION: What is a Job Track?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Job track definition, track explanation, MERN vs Java, track selection
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* What is a job track in NxtWave Intensive?
* Job track ante enti?
* What does job track mean?
* Track ante em chestaru?
* MERN or Java — what are these tracks?
* Job track oka course aa?
* What is the difference between job tracks?
* Track select cheyyadam ela?
* Job track ki meaning enti?
* When do I choose my job track?
* Give me a suggestion for selecting a course
* Course select cheyyataniki suggestion ivvandi
* Which course should I pick, any advice?
* Naku course selection lo help kavali
ANSWER: A Job Track is the specialized learning path you follow in the program, designed to prepare you for a specific type of software development role. In NxtWave Intensive, you do not choose your job track immediately on Day 1. Here is how it works: Growth Cycle 1 (Fundamentals) is the same for everyone — all students complete the same 4 courses regardless of which track they will eventually follow. This phase builds your core foundation. At the end of GC1, you take the Fundamentals Exam. Based on your performance, the system will suggest a job track for you. You can accept the suggested track or choose your preferred track by submitting the Job Track Preference Form. Available tracks: Online Program: MERN Full Stack, Java Full Stack Offline / Training Center: Python Full Stack with Gen AI, Java Full Stack with Gen AI Once your job track is activated, your personalized curriculum — GC2, GC3, and Placement Preparation — unlocks on your My Journey page at https://learning.ccbp.in/my-journey
TAGS: Job track, track selection, MERN Full Stack, Java Full Stack, Python Full Stack, Gen AI, Fundamentals Exam, track preference, GC1, job track form, track definition

QUESTION_ID: JT-004
CANONICAL_QUESTION: Why can't I see my Job Track on the portal?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Job track not visible, track not unlocked, My Journey missing tracks, GC1
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* My job track is not showing, why?
* Job track portal lo kanapadatle, enti?
* I can't see GC2 or GC3 on My Journey
* Why is my learning path incomplete on My Journey?
* Job track select chesanu but still not visible
* Track activate avvadam ledu, ela cheyyali?
* I submitted the job track form but nothing changed
* My journey lo only GC1 kanapadutundi, next cycles ekkada?
* Track preference form submit chesanu, next cycle unlock avvadam ledu
ANSWER: There are two common reasons why your Job Track may not be visible yet: Reason 1 — You are still in Growth Cycle 1 Students currently in GC1 will only see GC1 content on their My Journey page. GC2, GC3, and Placement Preparation become visible only after you: ● Complete all units in GC1 ● Take the Fundamentals Exam ● Submit the Job Track Preference Form This is by design. Until all three steps are done, further Growth Cycles will not appear. Reason 2 — You submitted the Job Track Preference Form but the track has not activated yet After submitting the form, your selected job track is activated from the next day onwards. If it has been more than 24 hours and your track is still not visible, please let me know will raise a support ticket on your behalf To check your current status, visit your My Journey page at https://learning.ccbp.in/my-journey and look for any pending units or notification banners. Or open this job track preference form if you have filled it already: https://learning.ccbp.in/course?c_id=2e09980b-9be7-415e-8d81-2c326efd59ff&t_id=452cff46-e7ee-4061-a846-dd84f7a0d697&s_id=c44a1731-9be1-42d5-9d07-4aad70d8cec8
TAGS: Job track not visible, track not showing, My Journey, GC1, Fundamentals Exam, Job Track Preference Form, track activation, pending units, Growth Cycle unlock, support QUESTION_ID : JT-005 CANONICAL_QUESTION: Can I switch my job track after selecting it? INTENT CATEGORIES: Track switching, job track change, MERN to Java, Java to MERN, track selection, backend difference QUESTION_VARIATIONS: ● Can I change my job track after submitting the preference form? ● Job track change cheyyachu aa select chesaka? ● MERN nundi Java ki switch cheyyachu aa? ● Java nundi MERN ki change cheyyachu aa? ● I selected MERN but want Java now, is it possible? ● Track change cheyyataniki option undi aa? ● Can I switch tracks midway through the program? ● Job track wrong ga select chesanu, change cheyyachu aa? ● Is it possible to change my specialization after choosing? ● I want to change my backend technology track, how? ● MERN and Java lo difference emi? ● Which track should I choose, MERN or Java? ● Backend lo difference enti MERN and Java madhya? ANSWER: Once you submit the Job Track Preference Form, switching to a different track is not allowed. It is important to think carefully before making your selection. To help you decide, here is the key difference between the two tracks: Both tracks cover the same frontend technologies — HTML, CSS, JavaScript, Bootstrap. The only difference is in the backend: ● MERN Full Stack — Backend uses Python, Node.js, Express JS, and MongoDB ● Java Full Stack — Backend uses Java and Spring Boot If you are comfortable with Python or have some prior exposure to it, MERN Full Stack may feel more natural. If you prefer Java or have a background in it, Java Full Stack is the right choice. Java will be more complex than MERN. Both tracks have strong placement opportunities. The choice should be based on which backend technology you are more comfortable learning, not on perceived difficulty — both are equally achievable starting from zero. Take your time to decide before submitting the form. Once submitted, the track cannot be changed. TAGS: Track switching, job track change, MERN Full Stack, Java Full Stack, backend difference, Python, Node JS, Java, Spring Boot, track selection, preference form, track comparison

QUESTION_ID: JT-006
CANONICAL_QUESTION: Can I get access to multiple job tracks?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Migration, version switch, Intensive 1.0, Intensive 2.0, program upgrade
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Multiple job tracks access cheyyocha?
* Can I learn more than one job track simultaneously?
* MERN and Java both access cheyyocha?
* Two job tracks parallel ga cheyyocha?
* Can I switch between job tracks?
* Multiple technologies oka saari lo learn cheyyocha?
* Java and MERN both tracks access untunda?
* Can I enroll in both MERN and Data Analytics?
* More than one track choose cheyyocha?
ANSWER: Access is provided only for the one job track you have chosen. To ensure effective skill building, we recommend focusing on one technology at a time — attempting to learn multiple technologies in parallel can lead to confusion and slow down your overall progress. The goal is to master one track fully and get placed.
TAGS: job track, multiple tracks, access, parallel learning, MERN, Java, Data Analytics, single track, focus Q: Why are HTML & CSS used in Power BI/Tableau, DA? ANSWER: HTML and CSS are commonly used in Power BI and Tableau to create custom visualizations and to customize the appearance of the data visualization. In Power BI and Tableau, HTML and CSS can be used to create custom visualizations and to customize the appearance of the data visualization, such as adding custom logos, background images, and customizing the layout. Additionally, Power BI and Tableau can import and use custom visualizations built in JavaScript and other web technologies, which requires a basic understanding of web development concepts like HTML, CSS and JavaScript. All these will not usually be covered in other training institutions. Our robust curriculum is why 1000s of companies hire NxtWave learners. Q: Why are HTML & CSS necessary for QA? ANSWER: Though CSS and HTML have few applications in QA or Power BI/ Tableau. The probability of getting a job becomes higher when you learn all these. All these will not usually be covered in other training institutions. Our robust curriculum is why 1000s of companies hire NxtWave learners. Q: What is the primary programming language you teach in the QA automation testing course: Python or Java? ANSWER: In our Quality Assurance track, we provide training in Selenium with Java. QUESTION_ID : MG-001 CANONICAL_QUESTION: Can I migrate from Intensive 1.0 or switch to Intensive 2.0? INTENT CATEGORIES: Migration, version switch, Intensive 1.0, Intensive 2.0, program upgrade, version change QUESTION_VARIATIONS: ● Can I move from 1.0 to 2.0? ● Intensive 1.0 nundi 2.0 ki switch cheyyachu aa? ● Migration window still open aa? ● I am a 1.0 student, can I upgrade? ● How do I switch to the latest version of Intensive? ● 1.0 to 3.0 migrate cheyyachu aa? ● Can I move to the current program from an older version? ● Version change cheyyatam possible aa? ● Old program nundi new program ki switch cheyyachu aa? ● 1.0 student ki migration option undi aa? ● Intensive 2.0 ki ela vellaali? ● Program version update cheyyatam ela? ANSWER: Migration from Intensive 1.0 to 2.0 is no longer available. The migration window closed in January 2025 and has not been reopened. Similarly, Intensive 2.0 is discontinued and is no longer open for new enrollments or switches. The currently active program is Intensive 3.0 (Online), which offers MERN Full Stack and Java Full Stack tracks, along with dedicated Success Coaches and live doubt sessions. TAGS: Migration, Intensive 1.0, Intensive 2.0, Intensive 3.0, version switch, migration window closed, program upgrade, discontinued, Q: Why is NxtWave Intensive effective for learning full stack development? ANSWER: NxtWave Intensive focuses on learning by building and practicing, rather than just watching lectures. Across both programs, the learning process typically includes: ● Concept learning through guided sessions or structured modules ● Hands-on coding exercises to practice every concept ● Building real-world projects step by step ● Access to mentors and domain experts for doubt clarification ● Mock interviews and resume preparation as you approach placement readiness This approach helps learners gradually move from learning concepts to building projects and preparing for interviews. You can read learner experiences here: https://www.ccbp.in/reviews Q: How many students will be there in each classroom? ANSWER: For the in-classroom program, each classroom usually has around 60 students. This allows trainers to guide the class through concepts, live coding sessions, and hands-on activities while keeping the sessions interactive. For the online program, learners access the curriculum through the learning portal and receive support from mentors, success coaches, and scheduled doubt support sessions. Q: Will class recordings be provided? Yes, learning resources are available through the advanced learning portal. Instead of providing exact classroom recordings, you will receive structured recorded sessions that cover the core concepts taught during the program. These recordings are designed specifically for revision and concept clarity. This allows you to: ● Revisit topics anytime ● Revise before interviews ● Strengthen concepts while practicing coding These resources remain accessible during your learning and placement preparation period. Q: Will I receive physical notes or study materials? ANSWER: All study materials, coding exercises, practice problems, and project instructions are available digitally through the advanced learning portal, which is used by learners in both the online and in-classroom programs. Q: Can I change my batch during the course? (Applicable only for Offline/in-classroom program) A: Batch changes may be possible depending on slot availability, but they cannot be guaranteed. We recommend continuing with your assigned batch to maintain consistent learning progress. In exceptional situations, a change may be considered if seats are available in another batch. Q: What is the eligibility to join Offline / In-classroom program? ANSWER: You are eligible if you are: ● A 2024 or 2025 graduate, or A final-year student graduating in 2026 ● Applicants should have 60% or above in academics. Students from any degree or branch can apply. Prior programming knowledge is not mandatory, as the program begins with fundamental concepts before moving into advanced topics. Online Program is Open to anyone looking for a software job (no specific academic cutoff enforced) Q: Can I switch between the online and in-classroom programs later? ANSWER: Switching between the online and in-classroom programs after enrollment is not allowed. Each format follows a different training structure, schedule, and learning environment. Because of this difference, learners are required to choose the program format that suits them best before joining. Q: How can I get my doubts clarified? Or How to get my doubts clarified by domain experts? QUESTION_VARIATIONS: How can I contact a domain expert? ANSWER: If student is from Offline Program: There are multiple ways to get help during the program. ● For in-classroom learners, you can ask questions directly during class and interact with trainers. ● Additional support is available through live doubt resolution sessions conducted by domain experts between 11 AM and 8 PM (Monday to Saturday). ● Also, you can always post your questions in the Discussion tab of the respective session/code practice. Domain experts will respond within 24 hours. This ensures that if you get stuck while practicing or building projects, you can get guidance from mentors. If student is from Online Program (Intensive 3.0): There are multiple ways to get help during the program. ● Additional support is available through live doubt resolution sessions conducted by domain experts between 11 AM and 8 PM (Monday to Saturday). ○ You can join these live sessions from Home Page - Live Sessions ● Also, you can always post your questions by clicking on Ask doubt present in the respective session/code practice. Domain experts will respond within 24 hours. ○ You can also see the doubts asked by other students in the Discussions tab below the session. This ensures that if you get stuck while practicing or building projects, you can get guidance from mentors. Intensive 1.0 or Intensive 2.0: ● You can always post your questions in the Discussion tab of the respective session/code practice. Domain experts will respond within 24 hours. Q: Can students from non-technical backgrounds join the NxtWave Intensive program? Or Do I need prior coding knowledge? ANSWER: Yes. Students from any degree or branch can join the program. Both the online program (8 months) and the in-classroom program (6 months) start with fundamental programming concepts, so prior coding knowledge is not required. During the program, you will gradually move from: Learning core programming concepts Practicing coding through guided exercises Building real-world projects These projects help you build a portfolio that you can showcase during interviews. Many learners from B.A, B.Com, B.Sc, MBA and other non-technical degrees have transitioned into software roles after completing the program. You can read their experiences here: https://www.ccbp.in/reviews Q: Can students from non-CS branches like Mechanical, Civil, ECE or EEE get tech jobs through this program? ANSWER: Yes. Students from Mechanical, Civil, ECE, EEE, Chemical and other non-CS branches regularly transition into software roles. Most companies hiring for entry-level developer roles focus on: ● Coding ability ● Problem-solving skills ● Project experience During the program, you will build several practical projects and strengthen your coding fundamentals, which helps demonstrate these skills during interviews. Many graduates from non-CS branches have successfully moved into software development roles after completing the program. You can explore their stories here: https://www.ccbp.in/reviews Q: Do I need a laptop to attend the NxtWave Intensive program? ANSWER: Yes, a laptop is required to learn effectively. While some sessions or quizzes may be accessible on mobile devices, you will need a laptop to: ● Practice coding Complete assignments ● Build projects This applies to both the online program and the in-classroom program. Recommended Laptop Specifications ● Operating System: Windows 10 / Ubuntu / macOS ● RAM: 8 GB or higher ● Storage: SSD recommended A laptop meeting these specifications will be sufficient for completing the program and working on projects. Welcome Session by Founder 2. Profile Details 3. Orientation 4. Start Learning Welcome Session Your onboarding begins with a 2-minute welcome note from the NxtWave Founders. This session introduces you to the program and sets the tone for your learning journey. Profile Details You will fill in your Basic Details, Education Details, Work Experience, Projects, and Achievements. This information will be shared with companies during your placement process. Orientation After completing your Profile Details, you will attend an Orientation Session. The Orientation Session is available in both Telugu and English. In the Orientation Session, you will learn what Intensive is all about, how your learning journey will be structured, and what types of support will be available to you throughout the program. Understanding these details will help you learn more effectively and make the most of the course. The Orientation Session is conducted live every Monday and Thursday from 10:00 AM to 11:00 AM. A recorded version is made available in the portal in case you miss the live session. If you miss the Monday session, you can watch the recorded session until 10:00 PM on Tuesday. If you miss the Thursday session, you can watch the recorded session until 10:00 PM on Friday. You must watch the Orientation Session completely (100%). Only after completing the Orientation Session will you be able to start your classes. Start Learning After completing the Orientation Session, your first class will start at 12:00 PM in the afternoon. If you missed the live Orientation on Monday or Thursday, watch the recording first. Once you click on Start Learning, you will receive your daily schedule to complete. The course follows a daily schedule. You are expected to complete your daily tasks by the end of each day. During the Orientation Session, you will also make a commitment to complete your daily schedule on time and stay consistent throughout the course.

QUESTION_ID: OB-010
CANONICAL_QUESTION: I enrolled today but my classes are showing as starting from yesterday's
INTENT CATEGORIES: Coding practice definition, hands-on exercises, practice sessions, coding
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Nenu eeroju enroll ayyanu, kani naku ninnati class nunchi start ayyay
* I enrolled today but classes are starting from yesterday's date
* Naa schedule yesterday nunchi start avtundi, enduku?
* Why does my schedule show a previous date when I just joined?
* Today join chesanu kani Day 1 yesterday date lo undi
* Enrolled today, but Day 1 session shows yesterday's date
* My schedule seems to start a day before I enrolled
* Class dates wrong ga unnayi, naa enrollment date kakunda
* I joined today but it looks like I am starting someone else's schedule
ANSWER: This is expected and nothing is wrong with your account. You have enrolled for the Monday/Thursday batch, and the orientation runs from 10:00 AM to 11:00 AM, with a recorded version available afterward — until Tuesday 10:00 PM for the Monday session, and until Friday 10:00 PM for the Thursday session. If you enrolled on a day after a live orientation (for example, enrolling on Tuesday and watching the Monday recording), you can complete both days' schedule in one go, as there will be a few introduction sessions, and be on the same page along with your batchmates. Your classes will continue forward from here as normal. If anything in your schedule still looks incorrect after this — for example, sessions appear locked or your completion status seems off — let me know and I can look into it further.
TAGS: enrollment date, schedule date mismatch, orientation recording, Day 1 confusion, classes starting before enrollment, Monday Thursday batch, recorded orientation, date confusion, combine schedule, batchmates Daily schedules and growth cycles What is the Daily Schedule? The Daily Schedule helps you stay on track by providing a structured day-by-day learning plan. The domain experts have already planned the curriculum progression(what to study, when to study, how much to study), so you do not need to create your own study plan. You simply need to follow the schedule provided in the portal. A specific portion of the curriculum is scheduled for you each day. What activities are included in the daily schedule? Examples of scheduled activities: ● Video Lectures : Concept explanations with guided learning. ● Cheatsheets : Short summaries for each topic to help you review and easily remember key points. ● MCQ Practices: Concept validation and self-assessment. ● Coding Practices: Hands-on programming exercises. Coding practices are completed using the Code Playground. ● Assignments : Concept validation and self-assessment. ● Course Exams : Conducted on a weekly basis at the end of each course to to assess your understanding and knowledge of the course content When are sessions unlocked? On Sunday we have a holiday so Monday to Saturday you will have the schedule. Daily there will be six hours of learning in total. Classes will be unlocked at 10 am IST daily. How to check your Daily Schedule? In this section we are going to see how to follow your daily schedule in the learning portal and when the sessions will be accessible or unlocked for you. 1. Firstly check if you are using the latest version of Google Chrome. If it is not updated yet, please update it. 2. Open Google Chrome and log in to the advanced learning portal(https://learning.ccbp.in/) with your registered mobile number and OTP. 3. You will find the "Your Schedule" section on the home screen. 4. There will be a date-wise schedule available for you for the entire program. 5. You will get the sessions, practice sets, and assignments to be completed on a specific day. 6. You can see them by clicking on the date in the calendar. 7. Under each session or assignment you can also see the duration, that is, how long it will take to complete the session in general. 8. For example here it takes 2 hours to complete the Arrays and Objects session and 3 hours to complete the assignment. 9. Click on the session to open the session. How do I mark a session as completed? 10. Once you complete the entire session you can click on the Mark as Completed button. 11. Then comes a popup where you have to click on Yes to confirm. Then the session will be marked with a green tick indicating that you have completed the session. 12. After completing the session you can give your feedback by clicking on the Submit Feedback button and if you have any doubts you can post them by clicking on the Ask Doubt button. 13. Sessions and assignments are usually auto-marked as complete once finished. 14. In the case of Cheatsheets, the reading material with all session-related keynotes, you can mark them as completed once you finish reading them. 15. Code Playground: You can also practice coding while you attend a session with the help of the code playground within the session. 16. Notes: You can take notes while watching the session with the help of notes. 17. You can revise the previous sessions just by clicking on the session of your choice from the table of contents present on the left pane. 18. If you want to go back to the home screen, just click on the arrow icon on top of the menu bar or the NxtWave logo. 19. If you have finished all the sessions and assignments on a given day, the day will be marked with a green tick. What happens if I miss sessions? 20. If you have completed a few and there are a few more to complete, then the green circle would be partially filled. 21. The sessions will be unlocked on a daily basis. 22. For example if a particular session is scheduled for tomorrow at 10:00 am, then it will be unlocked only at 10:00 am tomorrow. 23. Until then you won't be able to access it. 24. Make sure that you plan to stick to your daily schedule and complete them in the order that we have provided because a few sessions are built based on the topics that are covered in the previous sessions. 25. Skipping these sessions leads to a lack of clarity and confusion. 26. So strictly follow your schedule and complete them in the order given. 27. If you miss a few sessions for some reason, you can cover the pending topics first and then take up the day's schedule on the next day. 28. There won't be any sessions during the holidays and when you hover the cursor on these dates, it will show as a holiday. 29. If you have completed all the given sessions, you will get today's session in your schedule by default. 30. It means that you are up to date. 31. If you are not up to date, then by default you will get the day's schedule in which you have pending sessions in the Your Schedule section. How to watch sessions and access previous sessions? You can watch the previous sessions in a course in 3 ways: My Journey page: 32. Click on My Journey in the left pane. 33. You can find all the mandatory courses that are part of your program here. 34. Select the course in which you want to see previous sessions. 35. You can click on the specific Course button to access all the sessions in the course of your choice. 36. If you are watching the sessions on a laptop or a desktop you can see the contents on the left pane. 37. Select the session of your choice to start watching it. 38. For example, if you want to watch Loops concept present in Programming Foundations, you have to navigate to My Journey from the home screen and select Programming Foundation course in the Fundamentals Growth Cycle. 39. In the right pane you can select Loops from the table of contents. Daily Schedule: 40. You can click on the calendar icon on Your Schedule section and select the date on which the session is unlocked. 41. For example if you want to revise the sessions released on the 28th of the previous month, you can directly click on the calendar icon and select the corresponding date and you will be able to see the sessions unlocked on that date. Search Feature: 1. The search feature helps you quickly navigate to relevant content without manually browsing through courses. 2. You can access the Search option from the navigation bar at the top of the page or from the left pane main menu. 3. Use the Search feature to find any session, course, topic, unit, or cheatsheet by entering a keyword. Your search results will display all matching content available across the platform. How does growth cycle progression work? 42. Sessions in the next growth cycle will be unlocked only after completing all sessions, practice sets, and assignments in the current growth cycle. 43. If you build solid foundations, you can learn advanced concepts present in the next growth cycle easily. 44. The sessions in the next growth cycle will not be unlocked until you complete the sessions in the current growth cycle. 45. The sessions on these dates will be locked. 46. For example if your growth cycle 1 is going to be completed by 15th April but you have a few pending sessions that would take another 5 days for you to complete. 47. The schedule won't be unlocked from April 15th to April 20th. 48. That is, if you complete your assignments on 20th April, you will get new sessions from 21st April. QUESTION_ID : CP-001 CANONICAL_QUESTION: What are Coding Practices? INTENT CATEGORIES: Coding practice definition, hands-on exercises, practice sessions, coding after class QUESTION_VARIATIONS : ● What are coding practices in the schedule? ● Coding practice ante enti? ● Why do I have coding exercises after class? ● Practice sessions ki meaning emi? ● Coding practice mandatory aa? ● What is the purpose of coding exercises? ● Are coding practices graded? ● Coding practice skip cheyyachu aa? ● How are coding practices different from assignments? ● Session tarvata coding practice ela cheyyadam? ANSWER: Coding Practices are hands-on exercises that appear in your daily schedule after the video sessions. They are designed to help you immediately apply the concept you just learned — by writing actual code, not just watching or reading. Each coding practice is tied to a specific concept from the session. For example, after learning about Arrays, your coding practice will involve solving problems using Arrays. Why they matter: Concepts make sense only when you apply them. Coding practices are where real learning happens — not just in the videos. They build the problem-solving muscle that companies test in hiring exams. Assignments and advanced topics are built on top of concepts practiced here. Skipping them leads to gaps in understanding. Are they mandatory? Yes. Assignments are unlocked only after you complete the required classes, practice sessions, MCQs, and coding exercises. Skipping coding practices will restrict your access to assignments and exams. Recommended order per session: Watch the video session → Practice live using the Code Playground → Review the Cheat Sheet → Complete MCQs → Complete Coding Practice → Attempt the Assignment TAGS: Coding practice, hands-on exercises, practice sessions, mandatory, assignments, MCQs, session order, code playground, concept application, daily schedule

QUESTION_ID: CP-006
CANONICAL_QUESTION: After running my code, where can I see the test case results, including
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Python code run chesina tharvatha test cases fail ayithe ekkada display avthundi
* Where do I see test case results after running my code?
* Test cases pass or fail ekkada chudali code run chesaka?
* After clicking Run, where are the test case results shown?
* Failed test cases ekkada kanapadtayi?
* How do I check which test cases passed or failed?
* Code run chesaka results panel ekkada untundi?
* Test case output ela chudali Python lo?
* Run chesina tharvatha pass fail status ekkada untundi?
ANSWER: After you click Run, the test case results appear in a panel below the code editor — usually labeled Test Cases or Results. Each test case is listed individually, showing whether it Passed or Failed. For failed test cases, you can click on that specific test case to see the expected output versus your actual output, which helps you identify exactly where your code differs from what is required. If you do not see this panel, try scrolling down within the playground area, or check if your browser window is wide enough to display it alongside the editor — on smaller screens, it may appear as a separate tab or expandable section.
TAGS: test case results, where to see results, pass fail status, Run button, test case panel, expected vs actual output, Code Playground, Python results display

TAGS: Behind schedule, self-paced, learning pace, consistency, missed days, catch up, schedule lag, worried about schedule, company preference SECTION: My Journey & Growth Cycles My Journey is your personalized learning roadmap on the learning portal. You can access it from the left pane main menu. Your My Journey page displays your full learning path organised by Growth Cycles. Each Growth Cycle shows you the courses and topics assigned to that phase, along with the start and end dates, total duration in days, and your current completion percentage. Each course in your journey displays the relevant technology tags so you know exactly which skills you are building. A green tick indicates that you have completed a course. An incomplete circle indicates that a course is pending. You must complete all pending units in your current Growth Cycle before the next Growth Cycle is unlocked. If you have pending units, a notification banner will appear on your My Journey page with a View Pending Units button to help you navigate directly to the incomplete content. Your full learning path ends with a Mini Project, an Industry-Ready Certificate, and a Job Opportunity — all of which are unlocked progressively as you complete each stage of your journey. Growth Cycles Overview Your learning program is structured into four phases: ● Growth Cycle 1 (GC1) ● Growth Cycle 2 (GC2) ● Growth Cycle 3 (GC3) ● Placement Preparation (GC4) Growth Cycles 1 through 3 cover your technical curriculum, and Placement Preparation prepares you for job opportunities. Note : Students in GC1 will not yet see further Growth Cycles on their My Journey page — these are only visible after they complete GC1, take the Fundamentals Exam, and select their job track. Until then, only GC1 content is visible on the My Journey page. Growth Cycle 1 — Fundamentals Growth Cycle 1 focuses on Fundamentals that helps learners build core technical knowledge and professional skills before progressing to specialized Job Tracks. It is the same for both job tracks. During GC1, you complete 4 courses that are identical across MERN Full Stack and Java Full Stack tracks. At the end of GC1, you will take the Fundamentals Exam. Based on your performance, the system will suggest a job track for you. You can accept the suggested track or choose your preferred track — either MERN Full Stack or Java Full Stack. Once you submit the Job Track Preference Form, your selected job track will be activated from the next day onwards. Duration by Growth Cycle and Job Track The duration of each Growth Cycle depends on your selected job track, as shown below: Phase / Growth Cycle MERN Full Stack Duration Java Full Stack Duration languages, including Java. ● After mastering these concepts, you will proceed to Java-specific sessions, building on your foundational knowledge from the Python sessions. ● This structured approach will provide you with a more comprehensive understanding of programming concepts. Placement Preparation Intensive 2.0 and 3.0: Placement Preparation (GC4) is unlocked automatically once you complete all courses and attempt all course exams across Growth Cycles 1, 2, and 3. By the end of GC3, all course exams will have been unlocked — completing and attempting all of them triggers access to GC4. Intensive 1.0: Access to Placement Preparation is unlocked after either clearing the Python IDP L1 or completing 75% of the React course — whichever comes first. It does not appear in the daily schedule. It will be visible as a card in My Journey . Clicking the card redirects to the Placement Preparation page. The placement preparation is meticulously crafted to provide you with support, including resume building, offering interview preparation sessions, and conducting personalized one-on-one mock interviews to help you hone your interview skills. ● Orientation: Provides an introduction to the placement process and expectations. ● Interview Preparation: Helps you prepare for technical and non-technical interviews. ● Create Resume : Guides you in building a professional resume suitable for placements. ● Interview Readiness : Evaluates and improves your overall interview preparedness. ● Mock Interviews : Provides opportunities to practice interview scenarios before actual recruitment processes. Placement Preparation Link: https://learning.ccbp.in/placement-support Tip: Since recruiters will have access to your academic details, personal information, and work experience, it is crucial to ensure they are kept current. Please review and update this information in your Profile at https://learning.ccbp.in/edit-profile. Placement Opt-in and Activation At the end of Placement Preparation (GC4), you need to fill out the Placement Opt-in Form. This form will be available in your last day’s schedule under the name Placement Opt-in Form . After submitting the form, it usually takes 24 to 48 hours for your placement eligibility to be activated. Once activated, you will get access to the Placement Portal and can start participating in placement opportunities.

Once your job track is activated, your full learning path with all durations will appear on your My Journey page at https://learning.ccbp.in/my-journey. ANSWER — FOR STUDENTS IN GROWTH CYCLE 2 OR BEYOND: You can view the duration of all your Growth Cycles on your My Journey page at https://learning.ccbp.in/my-journey. Your My Journey page shows your full learning path organised by Growth Cycles, along with start and end dates, total duration in days, and your current completion percentage for each phase. QUESTION_ID: SEQ-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: Can I skip a few topics and directly attempt assignments or exams?
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Topics skip chesi assignments cheyyocha?
* Can I directly attempt the exam without completing all sessions?
* I want to skip ahead to assignments, is that possible?
* Topics complete cheyyakunda exam attempt cheyyocha?
* Why can't I access assignments without finishing classes?
* Can I jump to coding exercises directly?
* I skipped some topics earlier, what should I do now?
* Assignment lock avundi, topics complete cheyyala?
ANSWER: Assignments and exams are unlocked only after you complete the required classes, practice sessions, MCQs, and coding exercises for that topic — so skipping topics will restrict your access until the eligibility criteria are met. The learning path follows a deliberate sequence for a reason. Each topic builds on the previous one. The recommended order is: 1. Attend the class 2. Practice live during the session 3. Review the cheat sheet for that topic 4. Complete the MCQs 5. Practice coding exercises Following this sequence reinforces your understanding and builds the ability to apply concepts — both of which matter when it comes to actual assignments and company hiring exams. It's worth keeping in mind: the goal here is a job, and a good one. Rushing through half-understood concepts might feel faster, but it often leads to failing technical screenings and losing more time in the long run. Moving through the course properly — even if it takes a bit longer — gives you a stronger foundation and a better shot at clearing those exams confidently. If you've already skipped some topics, no need to worry. Simply go back to the topic or day you missed and pick up from there. Pending tasks are not a problem — learning correctly is what counts.
TAGS: skip topics, sequence, assignments locked, exam eligibility, learning path, recommended order, pending topics, course sequence SECTION: Early Access ANSWER: Early Access allows eligible students to unlock and complete an entire upcoming Growth Cycle (level) ahead of the standard daily schedule. It is intended for students who have prior knowledge, relevant experience, or can invest significantly more time (10–12 hours/day) into learning. Early Access is granted only at the Growth Cycle level — not for individual days or topics. When students request it, based on the reason, a support ticket can be raised. After completing the requested Growth Cycle, they can request the next the same way.

QUESTION_ID: EA-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: I already know these topics. Can I move on to the next topics or level?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Schedule acceleration, prior knowledge, skipping levels, self-paced fast-tracking
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* I already know this — can I skip ahead?
* I have prior experience with this content. Can I access the next level?
* These topics are too basic for me. Can I jump to advanced content?
* I don't want to wait for the daily schedule. Can I learn at my own pace?
* I want to complete the course faster. Is that possible?
* Naku ee topics already vastunnai, next topics ki vellachu aa?
* Naku ivi already telusu, munduku vellachu aa?
* I know programming already, can I skip to next cycle?
* Course slow gaa undi, fast gaa complete cheyyachu aa?
* Memu already ivi cheppindi, again cheyyadam waste of time ga feel avutundi
* I have more time, can I study more than the daily schedule?
* Can I complete two days' content in one day?
* Daily schedule kaakaunda complete cheyyadam possible aa?
* I want to learn faster, what should I do?
* Is there any way to unlock next topics early?
ANSWER: If you already have prior knowledge of the current topics, have relevant experience, or can dedicate more time to learning (around 10–12 hours per day), you may be eligible for Early Access. Early Access allows you to unlock and complete an entire upcoming Growth Cycle (level) ahead of the standard daily schedule — so you can progress at your own pace rather than waiting for each day's content to open. A few important things to know: ● Early Access is available only at the Growth Cycle level. It cannot be used to unlock just the next day's or next topic's content. ● For example, if your program has Growth Cycle 1, 2, and 3 — you can request Early Access for the full Growth Cycle 1 (Fundamentals) in advance, but not for individual sessions within it. To request Early Access: Send an email to support@nxtwave.tech from your registered email ID. Mention that you'd like Early Access and explain your reason (prior knowledge, experience, availability, etc.). Once you complete that level, you can send another email to request Early Access for the next Growth Cycle and continue the same process.
TAGS: Early Access, schedule, unlock, skip topics, prior knowledge, fast track, Growth Cycle, self-paced, level unlock, support request Early Access — Why Re-requesting Is Required for Each Growth Cycle GENERIC INFO (Additional context for RAG system) Each Growth Cycle has a system-enforced minimum duration (e.g., 50 days, 60 days — varies by cycle). The next Growth Cycle unlocks automatically only after this duration has passed AND the mandatory units are completed. Students who take Early Access often finish a Growth Cycle well before the system-enforced duration (e.g., completing in 30 days what is set for 50 days). In such cases, the system will not automatically unlock the next Growth Cycle — it will wait until the 50-day mark. This is why Early Access students must contact the Support Team again to manually unlock the next Growth Cycle. If a student has taken longer than the specified duration to complete the cycle, the next Growth Cycle unlocks automatically and no support request is needed.

QUESTION_ID: EA-002
CANONICAL_QUESTION: I completed my Growth Cycle under Early Access. Why isn't the next

ANSWER: Each Growth Cycle has a system-enforced minimum duration (for example, 50 days or 60 days, depending on the cycle). The next Growth Cycle unlocks automatically only after this time period has passed and all mandatory units in the current cycle are completed. When you take Early Access, you often complete the cycle faster than its set duration — for instance, finishing a 50-day cycle in just 30 days. In this case, the system still waits until the 50-day mark before auto-unlocking the next cycle. That is why the next Growth Cycle does not open immediately after you finish — and why you need to contact the Support Team again to request Early Access for the next Growth Cycle. What you should do: Send an email to support@nxtwave.tech from your registered email ID, mention that you have completed the current Growth Cycle and would like Early Access for the next one. Note: If you have taken longer than the specified duration to complete the cycle (e.g., more than 50 days), the next Growth Cycle will unlock automatically — no support request is needed in that case.

That specific topic can be accessed through other available courses on the portal. Progress completed there is reflected in the main program, so when the student naturally reaches that Growth Cycle, the topic will already show as completed with green tick marks. This is different from Early Access, which unlocks an entire Growth Cycle. This option is for unlocking individual topics in advance. RAG RETRIEVAL GUIDANCE FOR THIS SECTION INTENT CATEGORIES: Topic-specific early access, job preparation, interview readiness, unlocking future content, learning specific skills ahead of schedule. Common cross-query pattern: Students typically don't ask "how do I unlock a topic in advance" — they express urgency around a specific technology or skill needed for a job application or interview. These should map to this answer. Distinguish from Early Access (which is for full Growth Cycle unlock) — if the student needs only one specific topic, this is the relevant answer. Language handling note: Students may mention specific technologies like ReactJS, Node.js, SQL, etc. RAG systems should treat these as topic-specific unlock intents and map to this answer regardless of the technology named.

Focus on that and ensure your Placement Opt-in Form has been submitted if you haven't done so already. After submission, placement eligibility activation takes 24 to 48 hours, following which you will get access to the Placement Portal.

QUESTION_ID: FE-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: What happens if I fail the Fundamentals Exam?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Fundamentals exam, pass or fail, exam result, job track suggestion, exam
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* What if I don't do well in the Fundamentals Exam?
* Fundamentals exam fail ayithe emi avutundi?
* Is there a pass or fail in the Fundamentals Exam?
* Fundamentals exam lo low score vasthe emi avutundi?
* Will I be removed from the program if I fail the exam?
* Exam badly chesthe job track vasatunda?
* Fundamentals exam result recruiters ki vastunda?
* Will my exam score affect my placement?
* Exam lo marks takkuva vasthe emi avutundi?
* Is the Fundamentals Exam graded?
* What is the purpose of the Fundamentals Exam?
* Exam attempt cheyyakapothe emi avutundi?
* Fundamentals exam score share avutunda?
* Can I retake the Fundamentals Exam if I don't do well?
* Exam nervous ga undi, fail ayithe emi chestaru?
ANSWER: There is no pass or fail in the Fundamentals Exam. The exam is not designed to judge you or determine whether you can continue in the program. Its sole purpose is to assess your current level of understanding and help recommend the most suitable job track for you — MERN Full Stack or Java Full Stack. After you attempt the exam, our team reviews your performance and suggests a track that aligns with your strengths. You can accept the suggested track or choose your preferred track by submitting the Job Track Preference Form. A few important things to know: The Fundamentals Exam report will not be shared with recruiters or companies at any point. It is strictly used internally to guide your learning path. Completing the four Fundamentals courses is mandatory before you can attempt the exam — Build Your Own Static Website, Build Your Own Responsive Website, Python Programming Foundations (up to Functions), and Introduction to Databases (SQL). The exam is an opportunity to understand where you stand, not something to be anxious about. Focus on completing your Fundamentals courses thoroughly and you will be well-prepared.

What it is: It is a full, real-world application you build independently — demonstrating your hands-on development skills with React JS. Including it on your resume or portfolio shows potential employers that you can build production-level software and helps you stand out from other candidates. How to become eligible: To unlock the Mini Project, you must complete at least 90% of the required units in React JS — specifically, completing 5 assignments in the React JS course. Projects you may be assigned include: - Movies App (Netflix / Amazon Prime Clone) - Covid Dashboard - Tasty Kitchens (Swiggy / Zomato Clone) - Spotify Remix (Spotify Clone) - Mini Games (Collection of Memory Games) - Task Manager (Trello Clone) - Github Profile Visualizer (and others from the project pool) How submission works: Once you build and submit your project, it is evaluated automatically through test cases directly on the portal. Your submission is accepted once all test cases pass. Why it matters for placement: Adding a completed mini project to your resume significantly improves your chances of getting noticed by recruiters and clearing initial screening rounds. It is one of the strongest signals to employers that you have practical, hands-on experience — not just theoretical knowledge.

QUESTION_ID: CODE-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: How can I solve coding practices better?
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Coding problems solve cheyyatam ela?
* I'm struggling with coding assignments, what should I do?

* I can't solve the coding problems, help cheyandi

ANSWER: Here are the steps to approach any coding problem effectively: 1. Read and understand the problem clearly before writing any code 2. Break it down — split the problem into smaller, manageable parts 3. Identify the concepts involved and analyze the right approach for that problem 4. Write an algorithm — plan the steps before coding 5. Write clean code based on your algorithm The more you practice, the better you get. If you have subject-related doubts while solving a coding practice, post your question in the Discussion tab of that specific session or coding practice. Domain experts will respond within 24 hours.

QUESTION_ID: CODE-002
CANONICAL_QUESTION: Will unlocking solutions for coding practice affect my placement?
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Coding practice solutions unlock chesthe placement affect avutunda?
* Solution unlock cheyyatam wrong aa?

ANSWER: Unlocking solutions does not directly affect your placement eligibility or Skill Profile. However, it significantly impacts your ability to develop real problem-solving skills — which is exactly what employers test during technical interviews and assessments. If you rely on unlocked solutions without genuinely attempting the problem first, you miss the opportunity to learn from your mistakes and build the independent problem-solving ability that companies look for. During the placement process, you will be expected to solve problems on your own — so the habit of unlocking solutions early can hurt your actual interview performance. We strongly recommend attempting every problem independently first, using the AI Tutor for hints if needed, and only reviewing solutions after making a genuine effort.

ANSWER: This is intentional and here is the reasoning behind it. Programming Foundations with Python is already completed before starting Java Fundamentals. Python is used to teach you the core fundamentals of programming such as variables, loops, functions, conditions, and more. These concepts are universal across all programming languages. The logic is the same, only the syntax changes. Think of it this way — "hello" in English, "namaskaram" in Telugu, "vanakkam" in Tamil. The meaning is identical, only the way you say it differs. Programming works the same way. A loop in Python and a loop in Java do the same thing — the difference is only in how you write it. What takes 3 lines in Python might take 10 lines in Java, but the underlying concept is identical. Since you have already learned and understood these core concepts thoroughly during the Python sessions, there is no need to re-explain the concept again in Java. Instead, what you need to learn is simply how to write the same concept in Java syntax — and that is exactly what the cheatsheets cover for those topics. For any concepts that are unique to Java and not covered during Python, full video sessions are provided. So the curriculum is not incomplete — it is structured this way to avoid repetition, save your time, and build on what you already know rather than starting from scratch. If you feel you need more clarity on any specific Java concept, you can post your doubt in the Discussion tab of that session or join a Live Session (11 AM – 8 PM, Monday to Saturday) for guidance from domain experts.
TAGS: Java videos missing, Java cheatsheet, Python to Java, Java fundamentals, no video, syntax difference, programming concepts, loops, Java curriculum, incomplete curriculum, concept repetition, Python foundations, Java sessions, cheatsheet only topics

QUESTION_ID: JV-002
CANONICAL_QUESTION: Java course videos are not available in Telugu, but sessions for other
INTENT CATEGORIES: Pausing schedule, resuming schedule, rescheduling missed classes, pending
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Java videos telugu lo levu But other languages lo unnayi
* Java sessions Telugu lo ledu enduku
* Why is Java not in Telugu like other courses
* Java course Telugu support ledha
ANSWER: A few sessions are intentionally kept in English — and here is why this matters for you. In companies, everyone comes from different regions, and English is the common language. Interviews and all selection rounds are conducted in English. India produces 15 lakh engineers every year, and communication is what helps the best stand out — 7 in 10 Indian tech recruiters say communication is the number one reason a final-round candidate does not convert, not technical skills. That is why these sessions are in English: so you start practicing now, because fluency does not come suddenly during interviews. If you have a doubt in any session, you can ask it in Telugu or Tenglish during Live Sessions — the mentor will respond in simple English or Tenglish to help you follow along.
TAGS: Java Telugu, language availability, English sessions intentional, communication skills, interview readiness, Live Sessions, Tenglish doubts Pause Learning Feature Intent categories: Pausing schedule, resuming schedule, rescheduling missed classes, pending tasks due to personal reasons, managing backlog TAGS: Pause learning, resume learning, schedule pause, pending tasks, backlog, catch up, rescheduled classes, missed sessions, pause limit, 90 days, exams, health, travel, family function, early resume, daily schedule The Pause Learning feature allows you to temporarily pause your daily schedule when you are unable to continue due to personal circumstances. You can access the Pause Learning option by clicking on your profile icon at the bottom left of the left navigation pane and selecting Pause Learning from the menu. How to Pause Your Learning When you click Pause Learning, a dialog box will appear asking you to fill in three fields: Start Date: Select the first date from which you want to pause your schedule. End Date: Select the last date of your pause period. Reason: Select the reason for pausing from the dropdown. The available reasons are: Exams, Health, Need to finish pending videos or practices, College assignments or activities, Family functions or event, Travelling, Network or device issues, and Other. Once you fill in all three fields, click the Pause button to confirm. What Happens When You Pause When you pause your schedule, new sessions will not be unlocked for the paused dates. The daily classes scheduled for those paused dates will not be accessible from their original dates. A pause symbol will appear on those dates in your schedule.You will be able to access the previous sessions. You have an option to resume your learning, if you wish to. Your paused classes will automatically be carried forward to the upcoming dates in your schedule. For example, if you pause June 1st and June 2nd, the June 1st class will appear on June 3rd and the June 2nd class will appear on June 4th automatically. You can access the paused classes through the rescheduled dates going forward. Pausing your schedule does not affect your access to any previously completed classes, course exams, live sessions, or any other content on the portal. Only the specific paused days are skipped. All previously available content remains accessible as usual. Pause Duration Limit You can use the Pause option for as many days as needed, such as 2 days, 3 days, or longer periods. However, the maximum total pause duration allowed across the entire course is 90 days. Once you reach the 90-day limit, the Pause option will no longer be available. How Resuming Affects Your Course Access If you pause your course, your overall course access period will be extended by the same number of days. For instance, if you pause for 30 days, you will have access to the course for an additional 30 days beyond your original 16 months of access from the date of registration. Note: A maximum of 90 days can be paused during the program in total. How to Resume Learning If you paused your schedule for a certain number of days but your situation resolved earlier than expected, you can resume learning before your originally selected end date. When you resume, your schedule reopens on the same day, immediately — you don't need to wait for the original end date. Example: If you pause from June 1st to June 5th but resume on June 3rd: ● June 1st's class will appear on June 3rd (the first pending paused class) ● June 2nd's class will appear on June 4th ● Going forward, your remaining classes shift accordingly based on the day you resumed To resume early, go to your profile icon → select Cancel Pause → Confirm. Your schedule will reopen immediately. For example, if you paused for 15 days due to exams but your exams are completed in 10 days, you can resume your classes from the 11th day. When you resume, your schedule will reopen on the same day immediately. Click on Cancel Pause and Confirm, you will get the schedule For example, if you pause June 1st and June 5th, and resumed the learnings on June 3rd, then the June 1st class will appear on June 3rd and the June 2nd class will appear on June 4th automatically. You can access the paused classes through the rescheduled dates going forward.

ANSWER: Don't worry — falling behind due to unexpected circumstances happens, and there is a clear way to get back on track. The best approach is to first pause your schedule to stop new sessions from piling up while you catch up on what you've missed. Use that paused time to go back, review the concepts you've already covered, and solidify your understanding before moving forward. For example, if you have around 10 days' worth of pending content, pause for 10 days, clear your backlog during that time, and then resume — you'll be back on track without any additional pressure. How pausing helps: ● No new sessions unlock during the pause period ● Your upcoming classes get pushed forward automatically — nothing is lost ● You get focused, pressure-free time to catch up and revise ● Your course access period gets extended by the same number of days you pause, so you don't lose out on access time at the end of your program Once you've cleared your pending work, resume your schedule and continue from where you left off. Staying consistent from here is what matters most — you can still finish the course and reach your goals. To pause: Go to your profile icon (bottom left) → select Pause Learning → fill in your start date, end date, and reason → click Pause. To resume early once you're ready, go to your profile icon → select Cancel Pause → Confirm. Your schedule reopens immediately, the same day.

QUESTION_ID: PR-004
CANONICAL_QUESTION: I am unable to access paused sessions. How do I access them?
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Sessions not accessible after pause, how to access them?
* My paused sessions are not visible, what should I do?
* Paused sessions access avvatledu, ela cheyyadam?

* Pause chesina sessions resume tarvata ekkada untayi?
* Where do I find the sessions I paused?
* Can I watch my paused sessions before the pause period ends?
* Sessions are locked even after I resumed from pause.
* I resumed early, why can't I see my classes yet?
ANSWER: Paused sessions become accessible as soon as you resume — either naturally once your pause period ends, or immediately if you use Cancel Pause to resume early. Your paused classes are not lost — they are automatically rescheduled to upcoming dates from your resume date onward, not on their original dates. Example: If you pause from 1st Aug to 7th Aug but resume on 8th Aug, the 1st-7th Aug classes will appear starting from 8th Aug onwards. On the 1st-7th dates, you will see a pause symbol with the label "Your schedule is paused". ALWAYS share the above information first, regardless of what the student has already stated. THEN, check resume status from the conversation: - IF the student has already stated they have resumed / pause ended (do NOT ask again in this case): ask them to share the date on which they resumed, and direct them to check Your Schedule from that resume date onward. - IF resume status is not yet known from the conversation: ask the student to confirm whether they have already resumed (pause end date passed, or used Cancel Pause). IF the student confirms sessions are still not visible from the resume date onward: This is not expected. Offer to raise a ticket with their registered email ID and resume date for the team to investigate. Do NOT ask about resume status again if the student has already stated it. Do NOT offer a ticket before the student confirms the schedule is still not visible from the resume date onward.
TAGS: Paused sessions access, sessions after pause, resume sessions, pause schedule access, session visibility after pause, paused days rescheduling, same-day resume

Grade Percentage obtained in the exam Grade Points A 80%-100% 10 B 60%-79% 8 C 40%-59% 6 D 20%-39% 4 E 0%-19% 2 An overall cumulative grade will be assigned based on your performance across all courses within your chosen job track. The cumulative grade reflects your overall progress and success in the program. Note: Cumulative Grade = (Sum of all the course Grades present in the Job Track) / (Number of courses present in the job track) e.g., "For example, if you have 4 courses with grades 10, 8, 6, and 8, your CGPA = 32/4 = 8. Maintaining good grades throughout the program is crucial, as it can increase the number of job opportunities available to you. Recruiters will have access to your grades and performance during the program, and this information may be considered when evaluating candidates for job openings. Course Exams Portal Feature The Course Exams page shows all your course exams in one place. Course Exams page link: https://learning.ccbp.in/course-exams. Upcoming Exams — Home Page Upcoming course exams are also displayed directly on the Home page under the "Upcoming Exams" section, so you can quickly see and access your next exams without navigating away. ● Up to 4 upcoming exams are visible in this section at a time. ● Each exam card shows the exam name, scheduled date, and time slot . ● An Add to Calendar button is available to save the exam slot to your Google Calendar. ● A live countdown timer shows the time remaining until the exam window opens. ● If you have not yet attempted the exam, an Attempt button will be displayed. ● If you have already attempted the exam and wish to improve your grade, a Re-attempt button will be displayed. ● An exam card will remain visible in this section until you receive A grade for that exam. Once a grade is obtained, it will no longer appear here. To view and manage all your course exams, navigate to the Course Exams page. Exams are organized Growth Cycle-wise: Growth Cycle MERN Full Stack Course Exams Java Full Stack Course Exams GC1 (Same for both tracks) Build Your Own Static Website, Build Your Own Responsive Website, Introduction to Databases, Python Programming Foundations (Part 1, up to Functions) Build Your Own Static Website, Build Your Own Responsive Website, Introduction to Databases, Python Programming Foundations (Part 1, up to Functions) GC2 Python(From Recursions till the end), Build Your Own Dynamic Web Application, Developer Foundations, JavaScript Essentials, Responsive Web Design using Flexbox Python(From Recursions till the end), Developer Foundations, Java Fundamentals, Spring Boot, Build Your Own Dynamic Web Application, JavaScript Essentials GC3 Node JS, React JS Responsive Web Design using Flexbox, React JS ● Growth Cycles are locked until the previous one is completed, and unlock automatically once eligibility is met. Each exam card on the portal shows: ● Exam name and scheduled date/time slot ● Add to Calendar button - lets you add the exam slot directly to your Google Calendar ● Attempt button (for exams not yet attempted) or Re-Attempt button (for exams already attempted) - clicking this takes you to the exam ● Countdown timer showing how many days are left until the exam window opens (e.g., “02 Day to go!”) or a live timer if the exam is ongoing CGPA ● On the right side of the Course Exams page, you will find your Skill Profile panel. ● It displays: CGPA (Cumulative Grade Point Average) - shown as a circular gauge with your current score (e.g., 2.9 out of 10). ● Click “View Report” to see a detailed Performance Report. ○ For each course exam, you can review: Best Grade, Grade Points, Score ● Tier Badge - Your performance tier based on your CGPA. Tiers progress from Wooden -> Bronze -> Silver -> Gold -> Diamond. ● IRC Eligibility Note - A minimum CGPA of 7.5 is required to be eligible for the Industry Ready Certificate (IRC). The portal will display a reminder to retake exams if your CGPA is below 7.5. Test Guidelines Before The Exam ● Check Test Timings carefully, you can attempt the Test only during the mentioned time slots. ● Be ready with your Laptop/Computer at least 10 - 15 minutes before the test starting time. ● Ensure you have a Stable Internet Connection. ● Turn off notifications in your system/laptop and keep all your devices on Silent Mode. ● Please ensure that your device camera is working prior to the exam. ● Ensure that your laptop/computer is fully charged before attempting the Test. During The Exam ● Google Chrome is a recommended browser for attempting the Exams. You may face issues while using other browsers. ● As the exam is proctored you are not allowed to switch your screen, use a calculator, mobile phone, or any other electronic gadget. ● No other person should be present in the room throughout the duration of the test. ● It is recommended that you take the test in a well-lit area for effective proctoring. ● Please ensure that you are not looking away from the screen during the test. ● Do not seek any help from the internet or another person. ● Please ensure that you are not communicating with any other person during the test. ● If determined to be engaging in malpractice or not following instructions, you will be given two warnings before your test is terminated. To Avoid Technical Issues follow the below steps ● Before attempting the exam you must close all tabs and turn off all notifications ( Browser, App, and Device Notifications like Fully charged, plugged In, etc.) ● During the exam, do not press any special keys such as Windows key, F12, escape, etc. ● Before attempting the exam you should take Mock Exams ● Positioning of the web camera:- ○ Ensure that the web camera will capture your face from the neck upwards. Your entire face must be visible. Ensure that the position of it does not cut off part of your face. ○ If your webcam is built-in, adjust your monitor height if necessary and ensure that the webcam is pointing at your face. ○ If your webcam is mountable, position it on your monitor so that it is directly facing you. ○ If your picture is out-of-focus then you will get the below Error. Ensure that your face is clearly visible on the web camera during the exam. ● To help you prepare, please refer to the below images which shows an INVALID and a VALID exam attempt. ● Please check if your camera is working properly from any of these websites ○ https://webcamtests.com/ ○ https://www.onlinemictest.com/webcam-test/ ● Please check if your device is connected only to a single camera. Having multiple camera sources can cause an issue. You can disconnect any other camera devices before starting the exam (Having a built-in cam would be preferable). ● It is recommended that you take the test in a well-lit area. ● Check if any Antivirus Software / Extension is blocking the camera access. If yes, disable them. ● It is better to use a personal Laptop/PC instead of company/organization laptops ● Step 1: How to allow the camera access and resolve the cam issue ○ Open Chrome Browser and click on more option on the top right (⋮) ○ Click on Privacy and Security and then select Site settings later open Camera option. ○ Select the option that you want as your default setting. ○ Review your blocked and allowed sites. ● Step 2: How to Disable antivirus on our system ○ Click on Start button and search "Windows Security" ○ Select the Windows Security app from the search results ○ Go to Virus & threat protection, and under Virus & threat protection settings ○ Select Manage settings. ○ Switch Real-time protection to Off. Note:- Disable the remaining Antivirus & Securities as the same. Ex: McAfee … etc ● Step 3: How to Disable the extensions on our system ○ Open Chrome Browser and click on more option on the top right (⋮) ○ Go to Settings and select Extensions ○ Click on the Remove button on which extension that you want to remove. ○ Confirm by clicking Remove. ● Step 4: How to clear the cache & cookies And Clear browser history ○ Open Chrome Browser and click on more option on the top right (⋮) ○ Select settings and click on Privacy & Security option. ○ Choose clear browsing data and select time range All time to clear everything. ○ Select all the below boxes then click on Clear data. ○ Next go to "Cookies and other site data" and then select Allow all cookies then reload the current page. ANSWER: Sorry to hear that your exam was terminated. This can happen due to one or more of the following reasons: ● Switching windows or tabs — The exam gets terminated after 3 warnings if you move out of the exam window ● Pressing special/function keys — Keys like F1, F2, Escape, Windows key, etc. can trigger termination ● Device notifications — System notifications (including antivirus alerts, battery warnings, app notifications) can interrupt and terminate the exam session Please check whether any of the following pre-exam guidelines were missed: 1. Stable internet connection — Was your connection stable throughout? 2. Does your laptop fully charged? 3. Notifications turned off — Were all browser, app, and device notifications disabled before starting? 4. Mock Practice Test completed — Did you take the mock test beforehand to verify your setup? 5. Special keys avoided — Did you accidentally press any function keys or system keys? 6. Antivirus disabled — Was your antivirus or any security software turned off before the exam? If you followed all the guidelines and the exam still got terminated, I will raise a support ticket on your behalf. Our team will take 1–2 days to review your request and update you via your registered email ID. Could you please confirm the exam name and describe what happened so I can include it in the ticket? If any of the above guidelines were missed, please don't worry — Course Exams are conducted every week. Ensure you follow all the guidelines before your next attempt.
TAGS: exam termination, technical issue, notifications, special keys, antivirus, guidelines, support, course exams, window switch, proctoring

QUESTION_ID: GRD-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: How does the grading system work?
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* What is the grading system?
* How are grades calculated for course exams?
* Grade system ela untundi?
* Exam lo marks ki grade ela ivvtaru?
* What grades will I get based on my score?
ANSWER: You will have course-wise exams at the end of each of the courses. Grades will be given based on the scores obtained. For example, if you get 90 or above, you will get an A grade, and for scores between 70-80, you will get a B. These grades will be assigned for all scores between 0 to 100. An overall grade will be given as well for all the courses together. Also, the grades that you get during the course are not a blocker to becoming eligible for placements. A student becomes eligible for placements once they get a grade in all the course exams. But, having good grades increases your chances of getting placed as companies also have access to your grades and performance during the program.
TAGS: grading, course exams, placements, scores, performance

TAGS: zero marks, learning track, progress, re-attempt, course exams 4. Python IDP Exam [Python IDP Exam is applicable only for Intensive 1.0 students] Exam Details Q: What is the Python IDP Test? ANSWER: When hiring a candidate, companies expect them to have programming skills. The Python IDP test plays an important role in testing the candidate's programming skills. Here you will have Python IDP Level - 1 and Level - 2 tests. To qualify for the Python IDP Level - 1 test, you must finish growth cycle 1 and foundation exam 4, while for the Level - 2 test, you must pass the Python IDP level - 1 test (score at least 75). Q: How many times can I attempt the test? ANSWER: You can attempt it any number of times and improve your score. The best score among all attempts is considered. Q: How to attempt the Python IDP tests? ANSWER: Here you will have Python IDP Level - 1 and Level - 2 The Python IDP Level - 1 test details will be displayed on the placement preparation page. Whereas, Level - 2 test details will be shared with you on Announcements section or to your email id once you clear Python IDP Level - 1 (Score atleast 75). Note: 1. The tests will be accessible only during the scheduled times. 2. Attempting this Mock Practice Test is mandatory for every IDP exam. Q: What is the minimum score to pass the Python IDP Level - 1/Level - 2 Test? ANSWER: Your score must be at least 75 to pass the Python IDP Tests. Q: Will the coming IDP tests have the same questions? ANSWER: No, the questions will be different. Q: What is the importance of Python IDP Mock Practice Test? ANSWER: The Mock Practice test helps you to check whether your laptop/system supports the test environment, and you can experience the test environment. Notes: 1. Attempting this Mock Practice Test is mandatory for every IDP exam. 2. Be on the mock practice test at least a day before the actual test. 3. You need not worry about the completion status of the Mock Practice test as it is just for device checks. During the test, if you encounter any issues, please contact Support Team. We will review your response and help you take the test successfully without any issues. Q: How many questions will be there in Python IDP Level - 1? ANSWER: There will be four questions. Weightage is assigned to each question based on its difficulty level. Q: How many questions will be there in Python IDP Level - 2? ANSWER: There will be only one question. Weightage is assigned based on test cases for that question Q: How can I see my score? ANSWER: You will be able to see your Python IDP Level 1 test score on the placement preparation page. The Level -2 score can be viewed on the exam page once the test has been completed. Note: In 5 to 7 days following the date of the exam, we will run a plagiarism check on your code submission. Your score will not be considered if plagiarism is detected. Q: What is the pattern of Python IDP Test? ANSWER: Here you will have Python IDP Level - 1 and Level - 2 tests. The pattern of the Python IDP Level 1 test is as follows: - Eligibility: Finish growth cycle 1 and foundation exam 4 and python IDP practice series - No. Of questions: 4 - Duration: 75 min - Total marks: 100 - Pass marks: 75 The pattern of the Python IDP Level 2 test is as follows: - Eligibility: Clear the Python IDP Level - 1 test (score at least 75) - No. Of questions: 1 - Duration: 60 min - Total marks: 100 - Pass marks: 75 Q: Is a laptop mandatory to take Course Exams or Python IDP Tests? ANSWER: It is mandatory to have a laptop with a steady internet connection to take Python IDP Tests. Q: Is it okay to use a pen and paper or rough sheets during the test? ANSWER: Yes, you may use empty paper while taking the test. Q: My Python IDP test is not opening. What should I do? ANSWER: Please reload the page once and try accessing it. If you still have any concerns, please post your request through Live Chat here: https://bit.ly/3OoB7ui Q: Is it possible to take Python IDP Tests during pause? ANSWER: Python IDP tests can be taken even during the pause. Q: How do I become eligible for the Python IDP Tests? ANSWER: Here, you will have the opportunity to take the Python IDP Level 1 and Level 2 tests. To qualify for the Python IDP Level 1 test, you must complete the following: - Growth Cycle 1 - Foundation Exam 4 - IDP Prep Series Level 1 To qualify for the Python IDP Level 2 test, you must pass the Python IDP Level 1 test with a score of at least 75. Q: How to clear/crack the Python IDP Test? Solve the Python IDP Prep Series to improve your problem-solving skills even further. Check out the "Cracking IDP" course in Tech Foundation and attend problem-solving sessions we conducted to get a better understanding of the types of problems you might encounter. You can also practice previous Python IDP questions available here: https://bit.ly/40qMzvC. We hope these resources will help you succeed in the Python IDP. Good luck! Q: Why is my previous Python IDP score showing as Python IDP level 1? ANSWER: We have considered your previous Python IDP attempts as Python level 1 exam scores in our backend. Therefore, it appears that way. You don't have to worry about it. Note: Taking Python levels 1 and 2 is optional if you score at least 75 on the old Python IDP.
