Other Courses Other Courses (Bonus Courses) Other Courses is a section in the left navigation menu of the learning portal that provides access to supplementary learning content beyond a student's core curriculum (My Journey). The key distinction is: My Journey contains mandatory courses for the student's chosen job track, while Other Courses contains optional/bonus content. Link: https://learning.ccbp.in/other-courses Certificates are issued for all My Journey courses. In Other Courses, only the XPM (Exponential Performance Mindset) course has a certificate. The following courses are available by default under Other Courses for all job tracks: ● Programming: Problem Solving Patterns, Practical Python, Instruction Flows, Java Fundamentals, React Hooks, MongoDB, Database Concepts, Java Fundamentals, Programming Fundamentals ● Interview Readiness Exponential Performance Mindset (XPM) — includes certification ● Effective Communication for Job Readiness ● Professional Readiness — Aptitude ● Interview Kits - Python Interview Kit, Frontend Interview Kit Additional courses beyond the above defaults can be unlocked manually upon student request (for example, a MERN student needing Java content for an upcoming interview). These unlocked courses also appear under Other Courses. Some content is entirely outside the Intensive curriculum and cannot be unlocked — including Cyber Security, AI/ML, Competitive Programming, and DSA. For future My Journey courses requested in advance, refer to TU-001. RAG RETRIEVAL GUIDANCE FOR THIS SECTION Cross-reference: TU-001 (for unlocking a specific future My Journey course in advance). NL-001 (for locked content within My Journey). Disambiguation trigger: If a student asks for a course that may or may not be available, use the resolution flow below before escalating.

QUESTION_ID: OC-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: What is the Other Courses section and what is available there?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Other courses, bonus courses, supplementary content, extra learning, default
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Other courses section ante enti?
* What is available in Other Courses?
* Bonus courses ekkada untayi?
* My Journey tho paatu inka em courses untayi?
* Other courses lo em nerchukavachu?

* Default ga which courses available untayi?

* Portal lo extra learning material undi aa?

* Aptitude and English ekkada untayi portal lo?
* XPM course ekkada untundi?
* Interview readiness courses ekkada untayi?
* Other courses section ekkada untundi portal lo?
ANSWER: The Other Courses section is available in the left navigation menu of your portal. It gives you access to bonus and supplementary content beyond your core curriculum — content that is not mandatory but can give you a significant advantage in interviews and placement. The difference between My Journey and Other Courses: My Journey contains the mandatory courses for your chosen job track. These must be completed to progress through Growth Cycles and become placement eligible. Certificates are issued for all My Journey courses. Other Courses contains optional bonus content. You can explore these at your own pace alongside your main curriculum. Only the XPM (Exponential Performance Mindset) course has a certificate in this section. The following courses are available by default for all students under Other Courses: ● Programming: Problem Solving Patterns, Practical Python, Instruction Flows, Java Fundamentals, React Hooks ● Interview Readiness: Strategies and guidance to help you prepare for technical interviews and recruitment processes ○ Interview Readiness, Interview Preparation Strategies, Technical Interview Guidance, Mock Interview Preparation, Placement Support Materials ● Exponential Performance Mindset (XPM): Personal and professional development — growth mindset, productivity, and career readiness. Completing all four XPM courses earns you the XPM 4.0 Certification. ● Effective Communication for Job Readiness: Professional communication skills for interviews and workplace environments ● Professional Readiness — Aptitude: Aptitude, logical reasoning, and quantitative practice for placement assessments Note: Aptitude and English sessions are not mandatory for your core certificate, but they are very important for interviews. Make sure to complete them alongside your technical curriculum.
TAGS: Other courses, bonus courses, My Journey, supplementary content, default courses, XPM, aptitude, English, interview readiness, effective communication, Java Fundamentals, React Hooks, optional courses, certificate, left navigation

ANSWER: Aptitude and English sessions are not mandatory for completing your core curriculum or receiving your course certificates. You will still receive your certificate even if these remain pending. However, do not overlook them. Aptitude and English are tested in most company selection processes — written tests, reasoning rounds, and communication assessments are standard parts of hiring at many companies. Skipping them puts you at a disadvantage during placement. The recommendation is to treat Aptitude and English with the same priority as your technical sessions. Completing your curriculum 100% — including these — gives you the best possible preparation for interviews and makes you eligible for the Overall Course Completion Certificate. You can find Aptitude and English sessions under the Other Courses section in the left navigation menu of your portal.

QUESTION_ID: TM-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: What is the Timer feature in the learning portal and how do I use it?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Timer feature, Pomodoro technique, focused learning, productivity, study
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Timer feature portal lo enti?
* What is the stopwatch icon on the portal?
* How do I use the timer while studying?
* Portal lo timer endi untundi?
* How does the Pomodoro timer work?
* Can I set a study timer on the portal?
* Timer use cheyyadam ela?
* Focus ga study cheyyatam ela?
* Pomodoro technique ante enti?
* Portal lo timer icon ki meaning emi?
* How do I stay focused while watching sessions?
* Study time track cheyyatam ela?
* Timer set cheyyatam ela portal lo?
* Productivity improve cheyyatam ela?
* 25 minutes timer ante emi?
ANSWER: The Timer is a built-in productivity feature on the learning portal designed to help you study with focused, distraction-free sessions. You can find it in the top navigation bar while watching a session — it appears as a stopwatch icon. Once started, the countdown is visible at the top of the screen throughout your session. How to use the Timer: ● Step 1: Click the stopwatch icon in the top navigation bar while in a session. ● Step 2: A "Set Your Timer" dialog will appear. Set your desired duration (default is 25 minutes). ● Step 3: Click START. The timer will begin counting down and remain visible at the top of the screen. ● Step 4: Study with complete focus until the timer ends. ● Step 5: Take a short break, then start a new timer for the next session. Recommended approach — The Pomodoro Technique: The portal's timer is built around the research-backed Pomodoro Technique: ● Study for 25 minutes with total focus ● Take a 5-minute break after each session ● After every 4 sessions, take a longer break of 20 to 30 minutes You can adjust the duration to what works best for you — start with 25 minutes, observe your ability to focus, and modify accordingly. Tips for a distraction-free session: ● Keep your phone away before starting the timer ● Close all unnecessary browser tabs ● Let family members or friends know not to interrupt you during the session ● If an interruption is unavoidable and requires immediate action — stop the timer and restart when you return ● If the interruption can wait — postpone it until your timer ends Using the timer consistently builds a habit of focused learning. Students who follow structured study sessions typically cover more content and retain it better. Learn more about the Pomodoro Technique here: https://nxtwave-tech.notion.site/How-to-study-super-focused-using-the-Timer-and-the-powerful-Pomodoro-technique-c2ba74d474634f9194fb89ac0f3ef9e5

They run daily from 11:00 AM to 8 PM (Monday to Saturday) and cover curriculum courses such as HTML & CSS, Programming Foundations, React JS & React Hooks, Developer Foundations, Node.js, Introduction to Databases, and others. You can access them by clicking on the Live Sessions section on the right side of the home screen. Click on View More to see all available sessions. Each session displays the title, course name, date, time, session status (Live or Upcoming), and a Join Now button. A session appears in the list only when the corresponding course has been unlocked in the student's schedule. Even when a session is visible, the student must complete at least the first unit of that course before the Join Now button becomes active — until then, a prompt appears telling the student which unit they need to complete first. Upcoming sessions that have not started yet show an Add to Calendar option so students can plan ahead. The recommended session for the student's current progress is highlighted at the top of the list. After attending a session, students can submit feedback with the link provided in the zoom live session chat. ANSWER: There are two reasons why you may not be able to see or join a live session: Reason 1 — The course is not yet unlocked in your schedule. Live sessions are only visible in your list when the corresponding course has been unlocked for you. For example, on Day 1 of GC1, you will only see sessions related to Static Website. As you progress and more courses unlock — typically by around Day 10 of GC1 — sessions for those courses will also appear in your list. Reason 2 — You have not completed the required unit in that course Even when a session is visible, you must complete at least the first unit of the corresponding course before the Join Now button becomes active. Until you complete that unit, the button will be greyed out and a message will appear telling you exactly which unit you need to finish first. For example: If the HTML & CSS live session is visible but the Join Now button is inactive, you will see a message like — "Complete till Introduction to HTML > Introduction to GenAI in Frontend Development | Reading Material session to join." What to do: Go to your daily schedule, complete the required unit mentioned in the prompt, and then return to the Live Sessions section — the Join Now button will be active.

Where can I access the Gamification Feature? You can find this feature when you access Learning Portal Home page side menu How Gamification works? ● You just have to complete the sessions that you are getting in your daily schedule. ● Points are calculated based on overall learning activity including coding practices, assignments, MCQs, and video completions. When you get Points, you will get Coins . ● You will have Daily Goals & Weekly Goals to achieve and drive your motivation to practice more. ● You can also check your Daily Rank & Weekly Rank when compared with your peers in the group to improve your competitive spirit. What is Daily Goal & Weekly Goal? ● On a particular day, if you have any pending units unlocked till now (either units given in the schedule or any previous pending ones), you will solve them. ● Points ○ For each coding question you will get a Score ○ Points will be awarded based on your Score in coding questions ○ 1 Score = 1 Point ○ 1 Video unit = 100 Points ○ MCQ Practice, Exams = Based on total score, equivalent points will be awarded. ● Daily Goal ○ If you score a minimum of 100 points, your Daily Goal for that day will be achieved. ○ 1 Daily Goal = 50 Coins ○ If you are up-to-date with the schedule, then the Daily Goal is not applicable. ● Weekly Goal ○ Achieve Daily Goal at least 5 days in a week to reach your Weekly Goal ○ 1 Weekly Goal = 300 Coins ● Leader Board Based on the points, the daily & weekly leaderboards are displayed. You will get additional coins when you top the leaderboards. When achieved the 1st/2nd/3rd rank in the Weekly Goal Leader Board or Daily Goal Leader Board, a congratulatory banner will be shown ○ Daily Leader Board Rank in Daily Leaderboard Coins 1st 50 2nd 40 3rd 30 ● Weekly Leader Board Rank in Weekly Leaderboard Coins 1st 100 2nd 80 3rd 60 Is Gamification available in the mobile version too? Currently, Gamification is available in the desktop version. Going forward, it is in the plan to release a mobile version also.Gamification feature ante enti? ● What is the gamification system in the portal? ● How does gamification work in NxtWave? ● Portal lo gamification ela work avutundi? ● Points and coins system ela work avutundi? ● What are points and coins in the portal? ● How do I earn rewards on the portal? ● Gamification feature ekkada untundi? ● Portal lo rewards system enti? ● How is learning made fun in NxtWave? ● Assignments malli attempt cheyocha points kosam? ● Can I re-attempt assignments to earn more points? ● Assignment wrong chesanu, malli try cheyyocha points kosam? ● Assignment malli cheyyataniki points vasthaya? ● Assignments re-attempt cheyyocha? ● Assignment lo 2 answers wrong chesanu, malli attempt cheyyocha? ● Malli assignment cheyyataniki extra points vasthaya? ● Completed assig Note : Even if you solve the coding problems on your mobile, the score will be considered in the Gamification. QUESTION_ID: GM-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: What is the Gamification feature on the learning portal?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Gamification definition, feature overview, points coins goals, learning rewards
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* nment again attempt cheyyocha points improve cheyyataniki?
ANSWER: The Gamification feature makes your learning journey more engaging and rewarding by turning your daily coding practice into a game with points, coins, goals, and rankings. Here is how the full system works: Points: Points are calculated based on overall learning activity including coding practices, assignments, MCQs, and video completions. The more sessions you complete and the better your scores, the more points you accumulate. When you get Points, you will get Coins. Coins: Coins are earned by achieving Daily and Weekly Goals — not directly from points alone. Daily Goal: Earn a minimum of 100 Points in a day to achieve your Daily Goal. Each completed Daily Goal gives you 50 Coins. Weekly Goal: Achieve the Daily Goal on at least 5 days in a week to complete your Weekly Goal. Each completed Weekly Goal gives you 300 Coins. Leaderboard: Your daily and weekly points are compared with other learners. Topping the leaderboard earns you bonus coins — up to 50 extra coins for 1st place on the Daily Leaderboard and up to 100 extra coins for 1st place on the Weekly Leaderboard. You can access the Gamification tab from the left side menu on the Home Page of your learning portal on desktop or laptop. Assignments can be re-attempted. However, points for an assignment are awarded based on your score at the time of completion. Re-attempting an assignment after it is already completed may not award additional points — the system counts the activity as already completed. To earn more points, focus on completing pending coding practices, MCQs, and remaining schedule units.
TAGS: Gamification, points, coins, daily goal, weekly goal, leaderboard, store, rewards, coding problems, learning portal, desktop, left menu

* 300 coins ela vasatai?
* Am I on track with my goals?
ANSWER: Here is exactly how Daily and Weekly Goals work: Daily Goal: Earn a minimum of 100 Points in a single day by completing sessions. Once you cross 100 Points in a day, your Daily Goal is marked as achieved and you receive 50 Coins. Weekly Goal: Achieve the Daily Goal on at least 5 days within a week. Once you do, your Weekly Goal is completed and you receive 300 Coins. You can track your Daily Goal progress on the right side panel of the Home Page — it shows your current points for the day and how close you are to 100. A few important things to know: ● If you are already up to date with your schedule and have no pending coding problems, the Daily Goal is not applicable for that day. ● If you solve coding problems that were scheduled for a previous day, the points count for the day you actually solve them — not the original scheduled day. So if you complete Day 5 problems on Day 6, the goal for Day 5 will show as Unaccomplished. ● During a paused schedule, Daily and Weekly Goals are not applicable and coins will not be earned for those days. Resume your schedule to continue tracking goals.
TAGS: Daily goal, weekly goal, 100 points, 50 coins, 300 coins, goal completion, pending problems, paused schedule, goal tracking, home page, consistency, unaccomplished goal

ANSWER: Your Daily Rank is displayed on the Home Page next to your profile and is calculated based on your overall learning activity and performance on the platform for that day. Activities that contribute to your rank include coding practice completion, assignment completion, MCQ practice, star points earned, and general learning participation. The Leaderboard compares your points with other learners in your group. There are two leaderboards: Daily Leaderboard — based on points earned in a single day: 1st place — 50 bonus Coins 2nd place — 40 bonus Coins 3rd place — 30 bonus Coins Weekly Leaderboard — based on points earned across the week: 1st place — 100 bonus Coins 2nd place — 80 bonus Coins 3rd place — 60 bonus Coins To improve your rank, the most effective approach is to stay consistent with your daily schedule — solve all coding problems, complete assignments, and finish MCQ practices regularly. If you have pending schedules, completing a larger number of problems on a single day can also help boost your daily ranking significantly.
TAGS: Leaderboard, daily rank, weekly rank, bonus coins, rank calculation, coding practice, assignments, MCQ, competitive learning, points, top 3, rank improvement, home page

TAGS: my discussions, doubts, mentor response, central hub, track, discussion thread Nxtmock AI Interview NxtMock is a custom-built AI-powered interview practice tool designed to help students prepare for job interviews by simulating real interview experiences. It addresses the common challenges students face — nervousness, lack of confidence, and uncertainty about how to answer interview questions — by giving them a safe, judgment-free environment to practice as many times as needed. Where is NxtMock available: ● Build Your Own Static Website course — at the end of the course ● Programming Foundations with Python course — at the end of the course ● Placement Preparation Growth Cycle — multiple NxtMock AI interviews for every major topic Students can attempt each NxtMock interview up to 3 times per course, allowing them to refine their skills and improve their scores across attempts. How NxtMock works: 1. Watch the NxtMock AI Interview introduction video 2. Read the interview guidelines and click "I have read all the instructions" 3. Allow camera and microphone permissions (required for the interview) 4. The AI interviewer starts with an introduction round, then moves to theory questions, and finally to coding questions 5. For coding questions, screen sharing permission is also required so the AI can evaluate the code written 6. The AI evaluates all responses in real time and provides feedback during the interview 7. At the end, click "End Interview" → "Get Feedback" to receive a detailed, personalized AI-generated feedback report covering all sections (introduction, theory topics, coding performance) Feedback Report includes: ● Score and performance assessment per section ● Positives and areas of improvement specific to the student's answers ● Personalized suggestions to improve for the next attempt It is recommended to attempt NxtMock on a laptop or desktop for the best experience. A working microphone, camera, and stable internet connection are required.

TAGS: NxtMock, feedback, report, score, improvement, personalized AI Tutor (AI Mentor) The AI Tutor is an intelligent learning assistant available within selected coding practices on the Learning Portal. It can be accessed via Chat or Voice directly within the coding practice interface. Currently, the AI Tutor is available for a limited set of concepts across the courses, with more being added over time. The AI Tutor is designed to strengthen conceptual understanding and improve problem-solving skills through a guided-learning approach — it does not provide direct solutions or complete answers to coding problems. Instead, it helps students think through problems independently by providing hints, suggesting next steps, pointing out conceptual mistakes, and guiding them toward the correct solution. What the AI Tutor can help with: ● Explaining programming concepts clearly ● Clarifying coding logic and reasoning ● Helping understand errors in your code ● Explaining code you have already written — how it works, what concepts it uses, and the reasoning behind the solution ● Connecting coding practices back to concepts taught in the curriculum ● Guiding problem-solving approaches step by step What the AI Tutor will not do: ● Solve assignments or coding problems directly ● Reveal complete answers ● Bypass the learning process in any way The philosophy behind the AI Tutor is that developing the skill to solve problems independently is what ultimately prepares students for real interviews and the workplace — not memorizing solutions.

It brings together coding questions from across all curriculum courses into one place — so students do not need to navigate through individual course modules to find practice problems. Questions are organized by course and topic, and can be filtered by difficulty level (Easy, Medium, Hard) and completion status (Not Attempted, In Progress, Solved). Notes can be created directly while watching video sessions using the Notes panel on the right side of the session screen. All saved notes are stored centrally and accessible anytime from the left navigation menu or at https://learning.ccbp.in/my-notebook. The feature supports formatted notes with titles, bullet points, bold/italic text, links, and images. Cheat Sheets are pre-written by NxtWave's domain experts. They cover the key concepts from each session in a structured, standardised way. Every student sees the same cheat sheet. My Notebook is personal. It captures your own understanding, your own way of explaining things to yourself, things you found confusing, shortcuts you discovered, or connections you made between concepts. No two students' notebooks look the same. Research consistently shows that writing notes in your own words improves retention significantly more than reading someone else's notes — even good ones. So the practical value of My Notebook over Cheat Sheets is: You write it in your own words → you remember it better You capture exactly what confused you → you can revisit precisely that You build a personal reference → faster to scan before interviews than a full cheat sheet You stay actively engaged during sessions → rather than passively watching QUESTION_ID : NB-001 CANONICAL_QUESTION: What is My Notebook and how do I use it? ANSWER: Debug Mode is one of the most powerful features of the Python Playground. It helps you understand exactly what your code is doing — step by step — instead of just seeing the final output. How to use it: Open the Python Playground at https://learning.ccbp.in/code-playground/new, write your Python code, and enable Debug Mode. Once enabled, your code executes line by line. What you can observe while debugging: ● Variable Values: See the exact value of every variable at each line of execution — Line 1, Line 2, Line 3, and so on — so you know exactly how your data is changing. ● Scope Information: Understand which variables are local and which are global, and see which scopes are active at any point during execution. ● Function Execution: Track when functions are called, what they return, and follow the complete execution flow through your program. Debug Mode is especially useful when: Your code is not giving the expected output and you need to find where it goes wrong You are learning a new concept like recursion or functions and want to see how the program flows You want to build a deeper understanding of how Python executes your code

TAGS: Jobs portal, job openings, Open to Apply, Applied, Hiring Done, Previous Jobs, Check Eligibility, CTC, placement opportunities, application tracking, eligibility criteria, Placement Opt-in, left navigation, 3000+ companies, job listings, placement journey Certificates Certificates (Course Completion, IRC, and 30/50/100 Days Coding Challenge Certificates) NxtWave awards three types of certificates: Course Completion Certificates, the IRC (Industry Ready Certificate), and Coding Challenge Certificates (30 days coding challenge, 50 days coding challenge, 100 days coding challenge) Course Completion Certificates are awarded at the end of each course once a student completes all sessions, practice sets, assignments, and course items. Note: ● For Intensive 2.0 and 3.0 students, the course exam must also be completed. ● For Intensive 1.0 students, there are no course exams, so the certificate is issued upon completing all sessions. The course content is the same across all batches. Once a course is completed, the certificate is generated within 24 hours. A notification email with the certificate link is sent to the student's registered email ID. Students can also access their certificate directly from the portal via My Journey → select the course → Certification tab. Certification Tab Behaviour (My Journey): The Certification tab shows different content based on how much of the course the student has completed: ● Below 80% completion : The tab prompts the student to complete all course items before the certificate can be issued ● Above 80% completion: Only the remaining pending items are shown, so the student knows exactly what to finish ● Immediately after completing the course: A congratulations message is shown and the certificate is generated within 24 hours ● After certificate generation : The certificate is displayed with options to View, Download, Add to LinkedIn, or Copy the certificate link IRC (Industry Ready Certificate) is awarded upon completing the entire NxtWave Intensive program, after demonstrating skills through coding assignments, assessments, and real-world projects. [eligibility criteria for each program will be discussed in the next section] Course certificates are issued at the course level, not for individual technologies. For example, the Static Website certificate covers HTML, CSS, and Bootstrap together. Each certificate reflects a comprehensive skill set rather than a single topic. Note: Regardless of how many times you attempt the course exam, the course completion certificate is issued only once. Name on the Certificate The name that appears on your Course Completion Certificate and Code Challenge Certificate is automatically picked from the name you have entered in your profile on the portal (https://learning.ccbp.in/edit-profile). This is an automated process — whatever name is saved in the portal at the time of certificate generation will appear on the certificate exactly as entered. For example, if your full name is "Durga Prasad" but you have entered only "Praad" in the portal, the certificate will display "Prasad" and not "Duraga Prasad". It is important to ensure your full and correctly spelled name is updated in the portal before your certificate is generated. The portal has separate fields for First Name, Surname/Last Name, and a dedicated "Name on your IRC Certificate" field — please ensure all fields are accurately filled in as per your government-issued ID. If there is a spelling mistake in your certificate, please do not worry — it can be corrected and a revised certificate will be issued to you upon your request.

TAGS: certificates, 30daysofcode, 50daysofcode, 100daysofcode, course certificate, IRC, difference, certificate types IRC Industry-Ready Certificate (IRC) The Industry-Ready Certificate (IRC) is awarded upon successfully completing the entire NxtWave Intensive program, after demonstrating skills through coding assignments, assessments, and real-world projects. Unlike a regular course completion certificate, the IRC reflects practical industry-readiness — not just program attendance. NxtWave is an official partner of the National Skill Development Corporation (NSDC) under the Ministry of Skill Development & Entrepreneurship, Government of India. The IRC is a joint certification from NxtWave and NSDC, making it a recognized credential that signals practical skills to employers. IRC Eligibility Criteria by Batch: Intensive 1.0: ● Complete all individual courses ● Score at least 75 marks in the Python Course Exam ● Score at least 80% in each unit of the JavaScript Course Exam ● Complete the React Mini Project ● Complete the React Course Exam ● Clear the Mock Interview on Python and React Intensive 2.0 & 3.0 — MERN Full Stack: ● Complete all individual courses and receive all course certificates (a course is complete only when all sessions, practices, assignments, and mock tests are done) ● Attempt all course exams and achieve a cumulative grade of at least 7.5/10 ● Complete the React Mini Project with a minimum score of 90% and pass all plagiarism checks ● Complete Project Verification & Viva Intensive 2.0 & 3.0 — Java Full Stack, Data Analytics (DA), Quality Analyst (QA): ● Complete all individual courses and receive all course certificates ● Attempt all course exams and achieve a cumulative grade of at least 7.5/10 ● Complete the Mini Project with a minimum score of 90% and pass all plagiarism checks ● Complete Project Verification & Viva conducted by mentors Note: Grades are an important factor in IRC eligibility but not the sole criterion. Project performance, project verification, and viva performance are also evaluated. How to check IRC eligibility: Log in to the Learning Portal → My Journey → Industry-Ready Certificate (IRC) → view eligibility criteria How to request the IRC: Send an email to support@nxtwave.tech with your registered email ID and Phone Number. Alternatively, I can raise a support ticket on your behalf, please confirm. Once eligibility is verified, the certificate will be issued within 48 working hours via email from support@nxtwave.tech. Currently, the IRC certificate is not available to view on the Learning Portal — it is sent only via email.

QUESTION_ID: IRC-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: What is the Industry-Ready Certificate (IRC)?
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* IRC ante enti?
* What is IRC in NxtWave?
* Industry Ready Certificate gurinchi cheppandi
* IRC certificate ela vasthundi?
* What does IRC stand for?
* IRC and course certificate difference enti?
* Why is IRC different from a regular certificate?
ANSWER: IRC stands for Industry Ready Certificate. It is awarded after you successfully complete the entire NxtWave Intensive program and demonstrate practical skills through coding assignments, assessments, and real-world projects. Unlike a regular course certificate that confirms program completion, the IRC specifically indicates that you have built practical development skills and are industry-ready. NxtWave is an official partner of the NSDC (National Skill Development Corporation) under the Government of India. The IRC is a joint certification from NxtWave and NSDC, which makes it a credible and recognized credential for employers.
TAGS: IRC, industry ready certificate, NSDC, NxtWave, certification, course certificate difference

TAGS : 100daysofcode, 100 days challenge, completion form, form link, certificate, linkedin challenge, submit, 100 days coding challenge Technical Issues Technical Issues The following are common technical errors students encounter on the Learning Portal, along with the standard resolution approach for each. Sorry, You Don't Have Access to Any Program This error appears when a student tries to log into the learning portal (learning.ccbp.in) with a mobile number that is not associated with any registered program. When a student logs in with their correct registered mobile number — the one used at the time of payment and enrollment — the system identifies the account and displays the associated program automatically. If the mobile number is not recognised, the portal redirects to accounts.ccbp.in and displays the "Sorry, you don't have access to any program" error. The two most common causes are: the student is entering a different mobile number than the one used for registration, or a previously logged-in account on the same browser is being picked up instead of theirs. Resolution follows a two-step flow — verify mobile number first, then try Incognito mode — before escalating to a support ticket. Common cross-query pattern: Students reporting this error are almost always logging in with the wrong number or have a cached session from another account. Always check mobile number first before suggesting any technical fix. "Site Can't Be Reached" / "Trouble Completing Request" Error Errors on the screen: ● This site can’t be reached ● Something Went Wrong We’re having trouble completing your request This error is caused by a network or device issue on the student's end — it is not a server-side problem with the Learning Portal. Common triggers include unstable internet connections, DNS issues, outdated Chrome versions, corrupted cache/cookies, or network restrictions (such as organizational firewalls or ISP-level blocks). The resolution always starts with network checks and progresses to browser-level fixes before escalating to a support ticket. Blank/White Screen — Portal Not Loading When a student reports that the Learning Portal is showing a blank or white screen, the resolution follows two stages. ● First, ask the student to open an Incognito tab (Ctrl+Shift+N), turn off "Block third-party cookies" if shown, and attempt to log in again. ● If this does not resolve the issue, ask the student to clear browsing data in Chrome (Settings → Privacy and Security → Clear Browsing Data → set time range to All Time → enable all options → Clear Data), then go to Cookies and other site data, enable "Allow all cookies", disable remaining options, and reload the page. The student must also ensure they are using the latest version of Chrome. If unresolved after both stages, collect a screenshot or recording and raise a support ticket. "Temporarily Down" or "Getting Error" Message When a student sees a temporarily down message or a general error while accessing the portal or sessions (error: "The webpage at [URL] might be temporarily down or it may have moved permanently to a new address"), ask them to try accessing the portal on a different device (laptop or mobile), and if the problem persists, disconnect from the current network and reconnect using a different WiFi or mobile hotspot. Ensure Chrome is up to date. If unresolved, collect a screenshot or recording and raise a support ticket. "Register for the Program to Access the Course Content" Error When a student encounters a "Please register for the program to access the course content" error while accessing a specific course link, ask the student to share the name of the course they are trying to access and the access link if available. The bot should then check with the team and raise a support ticket to resolve access. Browser Not Supporting — Video Session Error When a student encounters a "Your browser doesn't support playing this video. Open in the latest Chrome browser" error while accessing video sessions, ask them to ensure they are using the latest version of Google Chrome and retry. If the issue persists after updating Chrome, collect a screenshot or recording and raise a support ticket. Video Session Buffering or Not Playing When a student reports that a video session is buffering or not playing, ask them to clear browsing data in Chrome (Settings → Privacy and Security → Clear Browsing Data → set time range to All Time → enable all options → Clear Data) and reload the page. Ensure they are using the latest version of Chrome. If unresolved, collect a screenshot or recording and raise a support ticket. Page Unresponsive While Writing Code When a student reports that the page becomes unresponsive while writing code, inform them that this typically happens due to errors in their code (such as infinite loops). Ask them to review their code line by line to identify the issue. If they are still unable to find it, direct them to post the issue in the Discussion tab of the respective session or coding practice — domain experts will respond within 24 hours. Heavy Traffic Error While Publishing Code When a student gets a "We are facing heavy traffic on our server" error while trying to submit or publish code, ask them to wait approximately 1 hour and try again. If the problem persists after waiting, collect a screenshot or recording and raise a support ticket. Biometric Face Verification The Learning Portal includes a Biometric Face Verification feature to ensure secure and authenticated access. Students are required to enroll their face once and authenticate using face recognition when logging in or accessing the portal. Setup Steps: 1. Click the lock icon in the browser address bar → go to Permissions → enable Camera 2. Follow on-screen instructions to enroll your face — ensure only your face is visible, no other faces, and lighting is adequate 3. On subsequent logins, click "Authenticate" when prompted, position your face clearly in the frame, and hold the device steady Common Errors and Fixes: ● Multiple faces detected — Ensure only your face is visible to the camera ● No face detected — Move closer and ensure your face is clearly in the frame ● Face not matched / Access denied — Contact support; another face may have been accidentally enrolled ● Reposition your face — Center your face fully within the frame in a well-lit environment ● Device in landscape view — Rotate device to portrait mode (mobile only) ● Camera permission denied — Click lock icon in browser → Permissions → enable Camera. For iOS: Settings → find the app → toggle Camera on ● Incorrect device time — Go to device Settings → Date & Time → sync to IST. Check current IST at https://time.is/IST ● Connection timeout — Move closer to the screen when "Move closer" appears, complete the process within the time limit, ensure stable internet and good lighting ● Something went wrong — Check internet connection and retry after 1–2 minutes; contact support if issue persists ● Camera Not Detected: For camera verification, test your camera at: https://webcamtests.com/ or https://www.onlinemictest.com/webcam-test/ General Rule for All Technical Issues: This applies to every technical issue query received. It must be followed strictly before any resolution steps are provided. Always follow this escalation flow — guide through self-resolution steps first → if unresolved, collect screenshot or recording → raise a support ticket on the student's behalf. STEP 1 — CLARIFY BEFORE RESOLVING Do not jump to a solution immediately. Students often use words that may mean something different from what is understood internally. For example, a student saying "portal not opening" could mean a white screen, a login error, an access error, or a network issue — these all have different resolutions. If the query is vague or unclear, ask a clarifying question first to identify the exact issue. Examples of clarifying questions: "Can you describe what you are seeing on the screen?" "Are you seeing any specific error message?" "At which step does the issue occur — while logging in, while opening a session, or while submitting code?" STEP 2 — REQUEST A SCREENSHOT OR RECORDING Always ask the student to share a screenshot or screen recording of the issue — even before suggesting a resolution. This helps confirm the exact error being faced and avoids resolving the wrong problem. Do not skip this step. STEP 3 — SUGGEST THE CLOSEST MATCHING RESOLUTION Once the issue is clearly understood from the student's description and screenshot or recording, match it to the closest known issue and guide through the self-resolution steps. STEP 4 — ESCALATE IF UNRESOLVED If the issue is not resolved after following the self-resolution steps, raise a support ticket on the student's behalf with the screenshot or recording attached. ESCALATION FLOW SUMMARY: Clarify the issue → Request screenshot or recording → Guide through self-resolution steps → If unresolved, raise support ticket

QUESTION_ID: TECH-001
CANONICAL_QUESTION: The learning portal is showing a blank or white screen and not opening.
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Portal open avvatledu, white screen vasthundi Learning portal blank screen chupistundi
* Portal loading avvatledu
* learning.ccbp.in open avvatledu
* Portal white page chupistundi, emi cheyyali?
* Website open kavatledu
* Portal open chesthe blank page vasthundi
* learning.ccbp.in white screen chupistundi
* Portal open avvatledu screen empty ga undi
* Portal open chesthe nothing show avvatledu
* Browser lo portal open cheyyataniki white page vasthundi
* Page load avvatledu, blank screen vasthundi
* Portal screen blank ga undi, emi jarigindi?
* learning portal not opening, only white screen visible
ANSWER: Please follow these steps in order. Do not skip any step — each one addresses a different possible cause. Step 1 — Try Incognito Mode Sometimes a previously logged-in account cached in your browser causes the portal to not load correctly for you. 1. Open a new Incognito tab using Ctrl+Shift+N (Windows) or Cmd+Shift+N (Mac) 2. If a "Block third-party cookies" option is shown, turn it OFF 3. Go to learning.ccbp.in and try logging in again with your registered mobile number 4. If the portal loads correctly in Incognito, the issue is with your browser's cache or cookies — proceed to Step 2 Step 2 — Clear Browser Cache and Cookies Cached or corrupted browser data is the most common cause of blank screen issues. 1. Open Google Chrome 2. Go to Settings → Privacy and Security → Clear Browsing Data 3. Set the time range to All Time 4. Enable all options (browsing history, cookies, cached images, etc.) 5. Click Clear Data 6. After clearing, go back to Settings → Privacy and Security → Cookies and other site data 7. Select Allow all cookies 8. Disable any remaining cookie-blocking options 9. Close and reopen Chrome, then go to learning.ccbp.in and try again Step 3 — Update Google Chrome An outdated Chrome version can cause rendering issues that result in a blank or white screen. 1. Open Chrome and click the three dots ( ⋮ ) in the top-right corner 2. Go to Help → About Google Chrome 3. Chrome will automatically check for updates — install if available 4. Relaunch Chrome after updating and try accessing the portal again Step 4 — Check Your Internet Connection A weak or unstable connection can cause the portal to load incompletely. 1. Open another website (e.g., google.com) to verify your internet is working 2. If the connection is unstable, switch to a different WiFi network or mobile hotspot 3. Try accessing the portal again after switching networks Step 5 — Try a Different Device or Browser If the issue persists on your current device or browser: 1. Try opening learning.ccbp.in on a different browser (e.g., Firefox or Edge) 2. Or try on a different device (mobile or another laptop) 3. If it works on another device or browser, the issue is specific to your current browser setup If none of the above steps resolve the issue: Please share a screenshot or screen recording clearly showing the blank/white screen, along with your registered mobile number, and I will raise a support ticket on your behalf.
TAGS: portal, white screen, blank screen, not loading, incognito, cache, cookies, Chrome, update Chrome, internet connection, different device, browser issue, learning.ccbp.in

QUESTION_ID: TECH-002
CANONICAL_QUESTION: I am seeing a "Temporarily Down" or "Getting Error" message on the
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Temporarily down error vasthundi
* Portal error chupistundi
* Website temporarily down message vasthundi
* Portal access avvatledu error vasthundi
* Webpage temporarily down undi

* Portal open chesthe error page vasthundi
* Website error chupistundi, access avvatledu
* Portal lo "webpage might be temporarily down" error vasthundi
* Site error vasthundi, portal load avvatledu
* Portal access cheyyataniki error vasthundi
* learning.ccbp.in error page chupistundi
* Portal getting error antundi, enti problem?
* Website down ga undi, ela fix cheyyali?
ANSWER: This error usually means the portal is temporarily unreachable from your current device or network. It does not always mean the portal itself is down — it could be a local network issue or a browser problem on your end. Please follow these steps in order. Step 1 — Wait a Few Minutes and Refresh Sometimes this error is caused by a brief, temporary server hiccup that resolves on its own. 1. Wait 2–3 minutes 2. Press Ctrl+Shift+R (Windows) or Cmd+Shift+R (Mac) to do a hard refresh 3. Check if the portal loads normally after refreshing Step 2 — Try on a Different Device This helps confirm whether the issue is specific to your device or a broader network/server problem. 1. Try opening learning.ccbp.in on a different device — a mobile phone or another laptop 2. If it loads on the other device, the issue is specific to your current device or browser — proceed to Step 3 3. If it does not load on any device, it may be a temporary server outage — wait 15–20 minutes and try again Step 3 — Switch Your Network Your current network may be blocking or failing to reach the portal. 1. Disconnect from your current WiFi or mobile data 2. Connect to a different WiFi network or switch to a mobile hotspot 3. Try accessing learning.ccbp.in again after switching networks 4. If the portal loads on a different network, the issue was with your original network — contact your internet provider if it continues Step 4 — Update Google Chrome An outdated browser version can sometimes cause error pages to appear even when the portal is accessible. 1. Open Chrome and click the three dots ( ⋮ ) in the top-right corner 2. Go to Help → About Google Chrome 3. Install any available updates 4. Relaunch Chrome and try accessing the portal again Step 5 — Clear Browser Cache and Cookies Corrupted cached data can sometimes cause error messages to persist even after the server issue is resolved. 1. Open Chrome → Settings → Privacy and Security → Clear Browsing Data 2. Set the time range to All Time 3. Enable all options and click Clear Data 4. Reopen Chrome and try accessing the portal again Step 6 — Try Incognito Mode 1. Open a new Incognito tab (Ctrl+Shift+N) 2. Go to learning.ccbp.in and check if the portal loads If none of the above steps resolve the issue: Please share a screenshot or screen recording of the exact error message you are seeing, along with your registered mobile number, and I will raise a support ticket on your behalf. Note: If the portal is confirmed to be down for all users, our team is already working on resolving it. In such cases, please wait for 30–60 minutes and try again before raising a ticket.

ANSWER: This error appears when our servers are temporarily under high load due to a large number of students submitting code at the same time. This is not an issue with your code — it is a server-side load issue. Please follow these steps in order. Step 1 — Do Not Refresh or Resubmit Immediately Refreshing or clicking Submit multiple times during heavy traffic can make the problem worse and may result in duplicate submissions or data loss. 1. Do not click the Submit or Publish button multiple times 2. Do not refresh the page immediately 3. Wait and let the current request time out naturally before trying again Step 2 — Wait and Retry The heavy traffic error typically resolves on its own once server load reduces. 1. Wait for approximately 30–60 minutes 2. Do not close the tab — your code is saved in the editor 3. After waiting, click the Submit or Publish button once and wait for the response Step 3 — Save Your Code Before Retrying To avoid losing your work during retries: 1. Copy your entire code and paste it into a notepad or text file on your device 2. This ensures your code is safe even if the page refreshes or the session times out 3. Once saved, retry submitting after the wait period Step 4 — Check Your Internet Connection A slow or unstable internet connection can also contribute to submission failures during high traffic periods. 1. Verify your internet connection is stable 2. If on WiFi, try switching to mobile hotspot or a different network 3. Retry submitting after switching networks Step 5 — Try in Incognito Mode If the error persists after waiting: 1. Open a new Incognito tab (Ctrl+Shift+N) 2. Log in to learning.ccbp.in with your registered mobile number 3. Navigate to the coding practice and try submitting again Step 6 — Clear Browser Cache and Retry Cached browser data can sometimes interfere with code submission. 1. Open Chrome → Settings → Privacy and Security → Clear Browsing Data 2. Set time range to All Time, enable all options, click Clear Data 3. Reopen the portal, navigate to the coding practice, and try submitting again If the error still persists after all the above steps: Please share a screenshot or screen recording of the error, along with the name of the coding practice and your registered mobile number, and I will raise a support ticket on your behalf. Note: If you are submitting during peak hours (evenings or weekends), heavy traffic errors are more likely. Trying early morning or off-peak hours often resolves the issue without any further action.

CANONICAL_QUESTION: I am getting a "Browser not supporting" error while trying to watch video
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Browser not supporting error vasthundi
* Video play avvatledu, browser error vasthundi
* Session video open avvatledu
* Your browser doesn't support playing this video error

* Video session open chesthe "browser doesn't support" message vasthundi
* Latest Chrome use chestunam kaani video play avvatledu
* Session video error vasthundi, emi cheyyali?
ANSWER: This error appears when the browser you are using does not support the video player format used on the Learning Portal. The most common cause is an outdated Chrome browser, but it can also be caused by conflicting browser extensions, incorrect browser settings, or using an unsupported browser. Please follow these steps in order. Step 1 — Update Google Chrome to the Latest Version The Learning Portal video player is optimized for the latest version of Chrome. Older versions may not support the required video format. 1. Open Google Chrome 2. Click the three dots ( ⋮ ) in the top-right corner 3. Go to Help → About Google Chrome 4. Chrome will automatically check for and install any available updates 5. Once updated, click Relaunch to restart Chrome 6. Go back to learning.ccbp.in and try playing the video session again Step 2 — Make Sure You Are Using Google Chrome The Learning Portal video player is designed specifically for Google Chrome. Other browsers such as Firefox, Edge, Safari, or Opera may not be supported. 1. If you are using any browser other than Chrome, switch to Google Chrome 2. Download the latest version of Chrome from: https://www.google.com/chrome/ 3. Install and open Chrome, then try accessing the video session again Step 3 — Disable Browser Extensions Some Chrome extensions — particularly ad blockers, script blockers, or privacy extensions — can interfere with video playback. 1. Open Chrome and click the three dots ( ⋮ ) in the top-right corner 2. Go to More Tools → Extensions 3. Temporarily disable all extensions by toggling them off one by one 4. Reload the portal and try playing the video session again 5. If the video plays after disabling extensions, re-enable them one at a time to identify which one is causing the issue Step 4 — Clear Browser Cache and Cookies Corrupted cached data can sometimes cause video sessions to fail loading correctly. 1. Open Chrome → Settings → Privacy and Security → Clear Browsing Data 2. Set the time range to All Time 3. Enable all options and click Clear Data 4. Reopen the portal and try playing the video session again Step 5 — Try in Incognito Mode Incognito Mode disables all extensions and uses a clean session, which helps identify if the issue is extension or cache related. 1. Open a new Incognito tab (Ctrl+Shift+N) 2. Go to learning.ccbp.in and log in with your registered mobile number 3. Navigate to the session and try playing the video again 4. If the video plays in Incognito but not in normal mode, the issue is with a browser extension or cached data — follow Step 3 and Step 4 above Step 6 — Check Hardware Acceleration Settings In some cases, Chrome's hardware acceleration setting can conflict with video playback. 1. Open Chrome → Settings 2. Scroll down and click on Advanced 3. Under System, find Use hardware acceleration when available 4. Toggle it OFF 5. Relaunch Chrome and try playing the video session again Step 7 — Try on a Different Device If the issue persists on your current device: 1. Try opening the video session on a different device (another laptop or mobile) 2. If the video plays on another device, the issue is specific to your current device's browser setup 3. Try reinstalling Chrome on your current device if all other steps have failed If none of the above steps resolve the issue: Please share a screenshot or screen recording of the exact error message, along with the name of the session you are trying to access and your registered mobile number, and I will raise a support ticket on your behalf. Note: Always use the latest version of Google Chrome on a laptop or desktop for the best experience on the Learning Portal. Mobile browsers may have limited support for certain video formats.

TAGS: cheat sheet, not marked complete, completion, redirect, navigation, tech team, course Refund Policy Refund Policy NxtWave offers a refund policy for the CCBP Intensive Program under specific conditions. The refund eligibility and process differ based on the payment model (Prepaid or Postpaid), the time elapsed since payment, and the student's placement outcome. Initial Refund Window — Within 7 Days from the Date of Payment: Both Prepaid and Postpaid students can claim a full refund of the Program Fee / Upfront Fee (including Seat Booking Amount, if applicable) within 7 days from the date of payment. The enrollment date is counted as Day 1. For example, if a student enrolls on June 1st, June 1st is Day 1 and the refund window closes at the end of Day 7. Refund requests on Day 8 or later will not be eligible under this window. To request a refund within 7 days, the student must send an email from their registered email ID to payments-support@nxtwave.tech with payment proof (payment receipt, payment ID, or transaction screenshot). After 7 Days — Prepaid Model Only: Refund may be claimed after 7 days if the student does not receive a job or receives a job with CTC less than ₹4.5 LPA within 8 months of completing the specialization, and has satisfied all Placement Support Terms and Conditions. The refund amount depends on the job offer received: if the CTC is between ₹3 LPA and ₹4.5 LPA, a deduction of ₹50, 000 applies; if no job or CTC below ₹3 LPA, a deduction of ₹25, 000 applies. After 7 Days — Postpaid Model: No refund is available under the Postpaid Model after the initial 7-day window. Intensive Offline Students: Offline students can claim a full refund within 3 days from the Batch Commencement Date by emailing payments-support@nxtwave.tech with payment proof. Post this 3-day period, all payments are strictly non-refundable. Refund Processing: Once a refund request is received with all required information, it is processed within 2–15 working days. A payout link is sent via Razorpay to the student's registered mobile number. The student must log in with their registered mobile number and OTP and provide correct bank account details or UPI ID. The payout link expires in 72 hours. Once the amount is credited (whether to correct or incorrect account), the refund request is considered closed. NxtWave is not liable for amounts credited to incorrect bank/UPI details entered by the student. Cancelling a Refund Request: If a student wishes to cancel their refund request, they must send a written confirmation email to support@nxtwave.tech stating: "I want to cancel my refund because I would like to continue my learning. I also understand that once I cancel my refund, I will not be able to apply for it again." Once cancelled, the student cannot apply for a refund again. Complete Terms & Conditions: https://www.ccbp.in/terms-and-conditions#Intensive-Terms

TAGS: refund, payout link, expired, 72 hours, Razorpay, support Placements SECTION 7: PLACEMENT SUPPORT 7.1 Is this a Job Guarantee Program? NO. NxtWave Intensive is NOT a job guarantee program. The program focuses on building skills and providing placement preparation support. Final job offers depend on the student’s skills, preparation, and performance in interviews. 7.2 What Placement Support is Provided? ● Aptitude and communication training ● Resume preparation ● Mock technical interviews ● Mock HR interviews ● Interview drives with 3000+ hiring partners ● Access to job opportunities matching student profiles ● Continued guidance to improve performance after rejections 7.4 Placement Eligibility When a student asks about placement eligibility, the bot must first identify which program version the student is enrolled in before giving a relevant answer. Program version determines the eligibility criteria entirely — giving the wrong version's answer causes confusion and incorrect expectations. Two types of companies come to hire NxtWave learners: Type 1 — Fullstack companies(Majority): These companies look for candidates who have completed the entire course and have a full-stack skill set. Completing the full course maximizes eligibility for this group, which represents the majority of hiring partners. Type 2 — Specific skill companies(Additional Support): Some companies come with requirements for specific technologies only — for example, Python + SQL, or SQL + JavaScript + React. For these companies, whoever has completed the relevant individual courses (and met the eligibility criteria) becomes eligible to apply, even if the full course is not yet complete. This is provided as additional support — it does not guarantee opportunities from all companies. You may be eligible for certain roles while not meeting the criteria for others, depending on company-specific requirements. The more courses you complete, the more companies you become eligible for. Completing the full course is what maximizes your placement potential across both types. Standard Placement Eligibility Process: As per the standard placement eligibility process, you are required to: ● Complete the entire course for your chosen job track ● Complete all course exams ((Intensive 2.0 & 3.0 only — course exams are not applicable for Intensive 1.0) ) ● Fulfill placement preparation requirements — resume preparation, mock interviews, and assessments ● Submit the Placement Opt-in Form Once all these steps are completed, you will be eligible for the companies that are looking for full stack skills. The majority of the companies come for full stack developers. For Specific-Skill Companies — Eligibility Threshold: ● Intensive 1.0: Complete at least 75% of each relevant course ● Intensive 2.0 & 3.0: Complete and attempt the course exam for each relevant course For a detailed understanding (Intensive 2.0 & 3.0): Video 1: New & Additional Placement Support Eligibility ● Telugu: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=j0RKzVIFNc4 ● English: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=umfR9WpuNWM Video 2: Complete Placement Process ● Telugu: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rirsdg9I_6c ● English: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0_1_2F2i_u4 When a Student Feels They Are Not Getting Enough Opportunities: ● [ Note : If a student has completed only Growth Cycle 1 (GC-1/GC-2/GC-3) and feels they are not receiving enough placement opportunities, the system should first cross-verify the student's current progress and then provide context-appropriate guidance.] ● For example, GC-1 covers: Build Your Own Static Website, Build Your Own Responsive Website, Introduction to Databases, and Programming Foundations with Python. At this stage, a student is only eligible for companies that specifically require HTML, CSS, and Python skills — which is a smaller subset of the overall hiring pool. ● Similarly, students who have completed GC-2 or GC-3 are eligible for a progressively larger set of companies, as their demonstrated skill set expands with each growth cycle completed. ● The bot should acknowledge this clearly and empathetically: confirm what has been completed, explain why the opportunities are limited at this stage, and strongly encourage the student to continue and complete the full course. ● Every month, hundreds of companies come to hire NxtWave learners. Students who complete the entire course and demonstrate their skills through offline assessments and mock interviews become eligible for all these companies, maximizing their chances of getting shortlisted. Companies prefer full-stack skilled candidates — completing the full program is what unlocks the majority of placement opportunities and significantly improves the chances of getting placed. ● Students who complete their full course early are in the strongest position to take advantage of placement opportunities. ● [Note: The system's recommendation in this scenario should always be: focus on completing the course first — your opportunities and chances will improve significantly once you do.] 7.5 Job Acceptance Policy ● When a student clears a selection process for a role through NxtWave’s placement system, they are generally expected to accept the offer ● This ensures fair distribution of opportunities across all learners ● Roles are matched to the student’s profile before recommending them for interviews 7.6 Applying for Jobs Outside NxtWave ● Students CAN apply for jobs on their own during the program at any time ● However, many students prefer to apply after completing most of the training (stronger projects, mock interview experience, resume ready)

QUESTION_ID: PLACE-044
CANONICAL_QUESTION: I added hyperlinks (certificates, projects, LinkedIn, GitHub, portfolio)
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* mam nenu konni hyperlinks pettaanu like certificates projects LinkedIn GitHub portfolio kani avi pdf form lo unte clickable avvatledhu
* hyperlinks PDF lo work avvatledu kani word document lo work avtunnai
* Will my resume links work for recruiters if PDF hyperlinks are not clickable?
* Resume hyperlinks ATS ki clickable avthaya?
* PDF lo links work avvatledu, recruiters chuda galara
ANSWER: This is most likely caused by how the PDF was generated or which app opened it — some PDF viewers, especially within messaging apps like WhatsApp, do not render hyperlinks as clickable even if the links are correctly embedded in the file. This does not necessarily mean the links are broken. When the same PDF is opened through Google Drive or a standard PDF reader (not inside a chat app's built-in viewer), hyperlinks are generally clickable and detectable by recruiters and ATS systems. To be safe, please test opening your PDF resume directly through Google Drive, a browser, or a desktop PDF reader rather than relying on the WhatsApp in-app preview. If the links still do not work outside of WhatsApp, share your resume file and I can raise a support ticket so the team can review the formatting.
TAGS: hyperlinks, PDF resume, clickable links, ATS scanner, WhatsApp preview, Word document, Google Drive, resume formatting, certificates, LinkedIn, GitHub, portfolio links QUESTION_ID : PLACE-045 CANONICAL_QUESTION: Will I be paid during the probation period, since the company mentioned CTC will be provided from day 1? QUESTION_ID: PLACE-046

TAGS: OTP not received, offline drive, interview registration, OTP resend, escalation, registered email Payment/Invite & Earn SECTION 9: FEES & PAYMENT 9.1 Online Program Fee ● Fee: ₹ 33, 000 after scholarship ● Scholarship: Up to ₹ 16, 000 savings based on eligibility 9.2 Offline / Training Center Fee ● Fee: ₹ 45, 000 ● Scholarship: Up to 80% scholarship available based on performance in the NxtWave Intensive Scholarship Test (NIST) 9.3 EMI Options ● EMI available via credit cards ● EMI plans shown during payment process based on bank ● Splits the program fee into smaller monthly instalments ● For EMI help, contact the admissions team 9.4 Refund Policy ● Governed by NxtWave Terms & Conditions ● Full details: https://www.ccbp.in/terms-and-conditions#payments ● Recommend reviewing before payment Q: What is the fee for the program? ANSWER: Online — ₹ 33, 000 after scholarship (up to ₹ 16, 000 savings based on eligibility). Offline — ₹ 45, 000 (up to 80% scholarship available through NIST — NxtWave Intensive Scholarship Test). Q: Is there an EMI option to pay the fee for NxtWave Intensive? ANSWER: Yes, EMI options are available for payments made using credit cards. You can select the EMI option during the payment process, where available banks and EMI plans will be shown. This allows you to split the program fee into smaller monthly payments instead of paying the full amount upfront. If you need help during the payment process, the admissions team can guide you through the available payment options. Invite & Earn NxtWave's Invite & Earn program allows enrolled students to refer friends and family to the Intensive Program and earn rewards for every successful enrollment. The referral portal is accessible directly from the left navigation menu in the Learning Portal under "Invite & Earn" . Referral Portal Link: https://nxtrewards.ccbp.in/invite-and-earn Every enrolled NxtWave student is immediately eligible to refer others and start earning rewards. For every successful Intensive referral, the referring student earns ₹3, 000 in rewards. Referral rewards are processed after the referred friend enrolls and completes the refund period. A 2% TDS deduction applies on the reward amount. How the referral works: 1. Copy your unique referral link from the Invite & Earn portal 2. Share it with anyone you want to refer 3. Guide them to fill out the demo registration form using your link 4. Once they enroll and complete refund period, you can redeem your rewards in the portal It is mandatory for the referred person to register using your unique invite link/code for the referral to be counted. If they register without your link, the referral cannot be claimed. Benefits for the referred friend: Instant scholarship worth ₹16, 000, 1:1 mentorship with success coaches, access to 3000+ companies via the placement portal, and direct access to offline placement drives. The Invite & Earn program also covers other NxtWave programs (Academy, NIAT) — the program can be changed at the top of the referral portal page to view those details. For complete terms and conditions including deduction details: https://www.ccbp.in/intensive-invite-earn-tc QUESTION_ID: REF-EARN-001 CANONICAL_QUESTION: How can I refer my friends or family to NxtWave and earn rewards? TAGS: Twitter, profile URL, submit, link, social media General 8. General Success Coach Every NxtWave Intensive 3.0 student is assigned a dedicated Success Coach to guide them throughout the program. The Success Coach is your go-to point of contact for non-subject-related queries, general guidance, platform-related questions, schedule doubts, certificate queries, and placement-related concerns. How to find your Success Coach: Go to the Learning Portal → click on your Profile (bottom-right corner) → select My Success Coach to view your coach's contact details. Available hours: 10:00 AM – 8:00 PM (Monday to Saturday) Support based on batch: ● Intensive 3.0 students can directly call their assigned Success Coach for any queries ● Intensive 1.0 & 2.0 students Dedicated Success Coaches are not assigned for these batches. However, I can raise a support ticket on your behalf and a call will be scheduled for you by the team. Could you let me know what you'd like to discuss? This helps the team prepare and assist you better during the call. When to contact your Success Coach: ● General guidance about the program ● Learning platform usage and navigation ● Non-subject-related doubts ● Schedule-related queries ● Certificate-related queries ● Placement-related doubts and guidance

TAGS: success coach, queries, guidance, platform, schedule, certificate, placement, subject doubts Teaching assistant The Teaching Assistant (TA) Program is a community initiative by NxtWave that allows current and past students to become mentors for fellow CCBPians by resolving their doubts on the discussion forum. It is designed to improve problem-solving, debugging, and communication skills while also enhancing a student's professional profile. TAs are required to resolve approximately 3 doubts per day on the discussion forum, which takes around 20–30 minutes daily. The program is flexible — TAs can take breaks when needed and make up for it later. How to join: Visit https://portal.ccbp.in/teaching-assistant and click "Become a TA" to access the assessment. The assessment is MCQ-based, testing how well you can respond to a student's doubt — no audio/video interviews are conducted. The assessment covers debugging, problem-solving, and doubt clarification skills. Benefits of being a TA: ● Personalised interview support during placement ● Improved problem-solving and debugging skills ● Teaching Assistant Fellowship credential for your resume ● Increased visibility to recruiters and HRs ● Access to a library of training resources and the mentor dashboard

QUESTION_ID: PRF-009
CANONICAL_QUESTION: I graduated in one year but cleared my backlogs in a later year. Which
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Backlogs clear chesina year graduation year aa?
* Resume lo graduation year emi pettali backlogs unna?
* 2019 lo graduated but 2021 lo backlogs clear chesanu, which year?
* Year of passing emi pettali resume lo?
* Backlogs tarvata clear chesthe graduation year change avutunda?
* Which year is considered as passing year if I had backlogs?
* Arrears clear chesina year resume lo pettali aa?
ANSWER: The year in which you clear all your backlogs is considered your official year of graduation/passing. For example, if you completed your degree in 2019 but cleared all backlogs in 2021, then 2021 should be mentioned as your year of graduation in your resume and profile.
TAGS: graduation year, backlogs, resume, year of passing, arrears, profile Skills/Work Experience

QUESTION_ID: PRF-011
CANONICAL_QUESTION: What should I do if I don't have any skills to add to my profile yet?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Portal expiry, access duration, 16 months, lifetime access, extension
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* Skills section lo emi fill cheyyali if no skills?
* Skills lekapothe profile incomplete avutunda?
* Skills mandatory aa profile lo?
* No skills yet, profile ela fill cheyyali?
ANSWER: The Skills section in your profile is optional. If you haven't built skills yet, you can leave it empty for now. As you progress through the courses and build new skills, make sure to update your profile regularly — recruiters review this information during placement evaluations, so keeping it current will work in your favour.
TAGS: profile, skills, optional, update, placement, recruiter QUESTION_ID : PRF-012 CANONICAL_QUESTION: Is my Profile on the NxtWave portal the same as my resume, or are they different? QUESTION_VARIATIONS: ● Mam profile ante nxtwave lo ma profile untundi adena mam Lekapothe resume aa ● Profile and resume same aa different aa? ● Is portal profile and resume the same thing? ● Naa profile resume kosam use chestara? ● Edit profile and resume different aa? ANSWER: Your Profile and your Resume are two different things. Your Profile (at https://learning.ccbp.in/edit-profile) contains your basic details, education, work experience, projects, and achievements — this information is visible to recruiters during placement evaluations and also feeds into your Skill Profile. Your Resume is a separate, formatted document created specifically through the Resume Building process (watching the Resume Building sessions and submitting the resume form for your track), which NxtWave prepares and sends to your registered email. Keeping both your Profile and Resume accurate and updated is important, since they serve different purposes in the placement process. TAGS : profile vs resume, edit profile, resume building, difference, Skill Profile, recruiters, placement evaluation Portal Access Portal Access & Expiry (INTERNAL ONLY, do not surface to students unprompted) Portal access is officially 16 months from the student's learning start date. Pausing extends the access period by the number of days paused. After 16 months, the portal expires. On humanitarian grounds, NxtWave provides access extensions to students who need more time — this is done out of courtesy and is not an official entitlement. Extensions are granted one month at a time upon request and team review. This information should only be shared when the student explicitly asks about expiry or extension — never volunteered proactively. The sequential flow below must be strictly followed. ESCALATION FLOW — FOLLOW IN ORDER. DO NOT SKIP STEPS. ● Step 1: Student asks about expiry → Give access duration and expiry date only. ● Step 2: Student asks about delay or extension → Reassure and redirect to learning. Do not mention the extension process yet. ● Step 3: Student explicitly says portal has expired or asks how to extend → Only now share the Request Access Extension process. ● Step 4: Student asks what happens after the 1-month extension expires → Only now explain they can request again. RAG RETRIEVAL GUIDANCE FOR THIS SECTION INTENT CATEGORIES: Portal expiry, access duration, 16 months, lifetime access, extension request, portal expired, access after expiry. Critical instruction: Never surface the extension process unless the student has first been told their portal has expired or they explicitly ask how to extend after expiry. Revealing extension options prematurely creates an expectation of entitlement.

ANSWER: You can access the portal for 16 months from the date you started your learning. You have started learning from {date}, access is going to expire on {date}. Note: Whenever you pause your course, your access period is automatically extended by the same number of days you paused — so paused time is never counted against your 16 months. What we strongly recommend is that you complete the course as soon as possible. Building your skills early puts you in a much better position to take advantage of placement opportunities and stay competitive. The sooner you complete, the sooner you can start your career.
TAGS: Portal access, 16 months, expiry, subscription, lifetime access, pause extension, access duration, start date

QUESTION_ID: PA-004
CANONICAL_QUESTION: My portal has expired. How do I get access back?
INTENT CATEGORIES: Portal expired, access extension, Request Access Extension, accounts.ccbp.in
QUESTION_VARIATIONS:
* My portal expired, what do I do?
* Portal expire ayyindi, ela access cheyyali?
* How do I extend my portal access after expiry?
* Portal lo login avvatledu, expire ayyinda?
* Access expired, extension ela teesukovali?
* Portal band ayyindi, ela open cheyyali?
* Can I get my access back after expiry?
* Portal expire tarvata ela continue cheyyali?
* Extension request ela submit cheyyali?
* Expired portal ki access ela vasatundi?
ANSWER: If your portal has expired, here is what to do: ● Step 1: Open https://learning.ccbp.in in your browser. If your portal has expired, you will be automatically redirected to https://accounts.ccbp.in ● Step 2: On that page, you will see your Intensive 3.0 card with a Request Access Extension button. Click on it. ● Step 3: A form will open. Fill in and submit the form. ● Step 4: Our team will review your request and, if approved, grant you one additional month of access.
TAGS: Portal expired, access extension, Request Access Extension, accounts.ccbp.in, expired portal, one month extension, form submission, team review, access renewal QUESTION_ID : PA-005 CANONICAL_QUESTION: What happens when the 1-month extension expires again? INTENT CATEGORIES: Extension expiry, repeat extension request, access after one month, portal access cycle QUESTION_VARIATIONS: ● 1 month extension expire ayyindi, emi cheyyali? ● Extension period expire avvadam taruvata ela? ● Can I request another extension after one month? ● Extended access expire ayyindi, portal ela use cheyyali? ● Extension tarvata malli access vastunda? ● One month extension complete ayyindi, next emi? ● Portal malli expire avutunda after extension? ● Can I keep requesting extensions? ● Extension period end ayyindi, ela proceed cheyyali? ● Access malli expire avutundaa? ANSWER: If your one-month extension period expires and you still need access, you can submit the Request Access Extension form again. Our team will review your request and determine whether to grant another month of access. To request again: Open https://learning.ccbp.in — you will be redirected to https://accounts.ccbp.in where you can submit a new request using the Request Access Extension button on your Intensive 3.0 card. We strongly encourage you to use every available day of access to complete your learning and secure a placement. Each extension is reviewed individually by our team. TAGS: Extension expiry, repeat extension, Request Access Extension, accounts.ccbp.in, access renewal, team review, one month, portal access, Intensive 3.0 External Job Portal Access After Intensive Program Expiry Some students are granted access to an External Job Portal — a community initiative that provides job opportunities to students outside the regular Intensive program enrollment. This is provided on top of the standard Intensive program access. When the Intensive program access expires, if a student has External Job Portal access, their experience on learning.ccbp.in will be different from the standard expiry behavior: ● The student will not be automatically redirected to accounts.ccbp.in ● Instead, on learning.ccbp.in, all features will disappear — courses, schedule, My Journey, etc. — and only the Job Portal will remain visible ● This can confuse students who may not understand why all their course content has disappeared In this scenario, the student's Intensive program access has expired but their External Job Portal access is still active. To regain full access to the learning portal (courses, schedule, etc.), the student must manually visit https://accounts.ccbp.in, find their Intensive program card, and click Request Access Extension . Once the team approves, they will receive one additional month of full portal access.
